## Current state (2026-11-03, pre-launch checklist re-check; SEO fixes; PageSpeed notes)

- Vercel "Functions Storage" was full: 239 production deployments had piled up (retention does not remove production ones). Owner deleted 236 by hand on 2026-11-03 (the Vercel CLI needs `--dangerously-skip-permissions` for DELETE). Now automated: `.github/workflows/vercel-cleanup.yml` runs daily, keeps the 10 newest, anything under 2 days old, anything building and the live deployment. Needs the repository secret `VERCEL_TOKEN` (steps in `OPERATIONS.md` 7f); not yet run for real, owner to do a dry run first. The selection logic was tested on fake data only.
- Accessibility 96 on PageSpeed found and fixed: the failing elements were the words of the `RevealWords` paragraphs (the fee tile and the "why Freetip" intro), which start dim (opacity 0.12, contrast 1.9:1 and 2.5:1) until scrolled into view, and a checker that looks early flags them. The dim start is now 0.5 (4.5:1 or better on both backgrounds, computed from the colour tokens); the reveal looks slightly less dramatic. Verified in a browser: all 40 words start at 0.5. Not verified: a new PageSpeed run (owner to re-run after the deploy).
- Privacy policy: one sentence added saying we ask YouTube which broadcast is live (for stream timestamps), policy date moved to 3 November 2026; text drafted for the Google OAuth verification justification (both scopes).
- Privacy policy: the Google API Limited Use sentence now uses Google's exact required wording (for the OAuth verification). Git history rewritten 2026-11-03 so every commit is under the owner's account only; local tooling files are untracked and git-ignored (kept on disk). Anyone with an older clone must re-clone.

- Owner confirmed Google Analytics is on (`NEXT_PUBLIC_GA_ID` set in Vercel). Checklist item 17 is done.
- Organization schema (`OrganizationSchema.tsx`) no longer needs a support email: it needs the legal name and writes the contact form page (`/contact`) as the contact point; an email is added only if one is set. Live after the next deploy.
- Unknown creator handles returned HTTP 200 (a soft 404) because `loading.tsx` starts streaming before the page runs. New `src/app/[handle]/layout.tsx` checks the handle before streaming, so unknown, badly formed and closed handles are a real 404 (checked on a local production build). Not yet deployed.
- New `public/llms.txt` (H1, one-line summary, links): fixes the one failing "agentic browsing" check (the old `/llms.txt` was served as an HTML page).
- PageSpeed (owner's run: mobile 78, desktop 82, accessibility 96, agentic 2 of 3). Lighthouse from this Mac on the live site: performance 96 mobile and 99 desktop, accessibility 100, SEO 100; the gap is probably Google's slower test servers. The 96 accessibility could not be reproduced: the owner should send the names of the failed accessibility checks from the report. Local lab LCP (simulated 4G, 4x CPU) is about 4 s on every page, while the real LCP is about 0.1 s; the likely cost is the four preloaded web fonts (about 225 KB, the Bricolage variable font alone is 131 KB because of its width and optical-size axes). Inlining the CSS (`experimental.inlineCss`) was tried and gave no gain, so it was reverted. Tried and reverted: dropping the display font's optical-size axis saved 53 KB and moved the local mobile score from 86 to 88 to 92, but headings came out about 5% wider (a visible brand change); not preloading the mono font gave nothing. Owner's second run (same day): desktop performance 96, mobile blocking time 530 ms and Speed Index 7.9 s, accessibility 96 from a "Contrast" item whose elements are not yet known (an axe contrast check at seven moments of the page load found none, so the owner should expand that row and send the element names), agentic 2 of 3 only because `llms.txt` was not deployed yet. Mobile blocking time is mostly hydration (about 500 ms of scripting in the shared React chunk at 6x CPU slowdown); deferring below-the-fold sections was judged risky because the pinned scroll sections measure page positions. WhatsApp preview: server side is correct (http redirects to https, OG tags present); a bare `freetip.in` is not turned into a link by WhatsApp itself, so share links with `https://`.

## Current state (2026-11-02, alert volume and layout built; alert studio item 4, the studio is complete)

- **Alert volume and layout built (alert studio item 4; run migration 0047 BEFORE the deploy; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Alert volume and layout" in a creator's Paid features box, "Alert box" group;).** A creator opens **Layout** in the dashboard (`/dashboard/layout`, nav link only with the switch on) and picks: where a picture placed "above" the card goes (above, left or right of the card) and a master volume for alert sounds and for the spoken voice (normal, 75, 50 or 25 percent), with a live preview of the real card on a landscape screen (it uses the creator's first picture if they have one; volume is not played in the preview). **Rules** are in `src/lib/alert-layout.ts` (pure, shared by the page, the save action, the overlay and the tests): the side is a key (left or right), the volumes are whole percentages from 10 to 100 and 100 is the normal alert so it is never stored, so a volume can only go down from normal, never above a sound's or the voice's own level; anything else is refused when saving and dropped field by field at play time. **Overlay:** the alert JSON carries `layout` (`src/lib/alert-extras.ts`, read only while the switch is on at play time, in its own try so a missing column only drops it); the sound's volume is its own volume (Sounds page, floor 10 percent) times the master, the voice's is the master (`mixVolume`); the side only changes the layout of media placed above, on landscape stages (a vertical stage keeps the picture above, and a picture behind the card or over the whole screen is unchanged). The picture is a flex row (`.ov-side`, `row-reverse` for right) next to the card, which keeps its normal width. **Storage:** `users.alert_layout jsonb` (migration 0047), null is the normal alert; `saveAlertLayout` (`src/app/actions/alert-layout.ts`) checks the switch and every field. **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/alert-layout.spec.ts` (12 tests: what may be picked, stored junk, the volume mix, the play-time switch, the loader against the real schema in memory including junk rows and a missing column) passes; four new browser tests in `test/overlay.spec.ts` (the picture sits left or right of the card with the layout and above it without; the voice plays at the creator's master volume) pass with the other overlay specs in installed Chrome against the production build (33 passed). **Not verified:** migration 0047 on real Neon; the Layout page and the save in a real signed-in browser; the side layout and the volumes in real OBS (OBS has its own audio mixer for the browser source, which also still applies); how the side layout looks with a wide or tall picture, a very long name or a level-3 big tip (the picture is held to 20 percent of the stage width and the stack to 96 percent). **Known limits:** volume can only be lowered; no separate volume per sound (that is the Sounds page); no layout choice for pictures placed behind or over the screen; the side layout is landscape only. **The whole alert studio (items 1 to 4: text and colours, motion, variations, volume and layout) is built.** Owner to do, at the end: run migrations 0045, 0046 and 0047 in Neon, tick the four "Alert box" switches for a test creator (plus custom alert media), try every page, send real test tips, and say what to change.

## Current state (2026-11-02, alert variations by tip size built; alert studio item 3)

- **Alert variations built (alert studio item 3; run migration 0046 BEFORE the deploy, after 0045; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Alert variations by tip size" in a creator's Paid features box, "Alert box" group;).** A creator opens **Variations** in the dashboard (`/dashboard/variations`, nav link only with the switch on) and adds "from this tip size up" entries (₹1 to ₹1,00,000, up to 10, one per size, saving a size again replaces it): a media file, a sound, their own headline and card, text and amount colours, and for ₹1,000 or more a longer stay (10 s to 5 min, same floor as alert rules). A live preview runs the real card at the typed amount with the chosen media and text (the sound and the stay are not played in the preview). **Which variation a tip gets:** the one with the highest size at or below the tip (`pickVariation` in `src/lib/alert-variations.ts`, pure, shared by the page, the save action, the overlay resolver and the tests). **What a fan's own choice always beats (the owner-delegated rule from the plan):** a tip that picked an alert rule on the tip page gets no variation at all (read from `tip_orders.alert_rule_id`, even if the rules switch is off at play time); a soundboard sound the fan picked replaces the variation's sound (its media, wording and stay still apply); a spoken (voice) tip has no sound, so the variation's sound is dropped. Order for the sound: fan's soundboard pick, then a rule's, then the variation's, then the creator's default. **Each part has its own switch at play time:** the media needs "custom alert media", the headline and colours need "alert text and colours" (the variation's colours sit on top of the creator's standard text field by field, are judged against the creator's own card colour, and if they do not read next to the standard colours only the wording is used), the long stay is checked again against the ₹1,000 floor. A variation's media that is not playable (removed, switched off) falls back to the normal rotation. The message card (after an approval) wears the variation's wording and colours (the headline stays alert-card only, as in item 1) but has no sound, media or stay. **Storage:** new table `alert_variations` (migration 0046: creator, size in paise from 100, media, sound, `text jsonb`, stay seconds; unique per creator and size; deleting a media or sound clears it from the variation; database checks mirror the rules). `saveVariation` and `deleteVariation` (`src/app/actions/alert-variations.ts`) check the switch, that files and sounds are the creator's own, and every field through the same rules the overlay uses; delete is always allowed. **Overlay:** no new fields; the variation resolves into the existing `sound`, `media`, `stayMs` and `text` of the alert JSON (`src/lib/alert-extras.ts`, read only while the switch is on, in its own try so a missing table only drops the variations). **Verified:** tsc and eslint pass; new `test/alert-variations.spec.ts` (30 tests: picking, the stay floor, merging and re-checking text, the form, every precedence rule, each switch, the message card, the loader against the real schema in memory including constraints, a picked tip, junk rows, a deleted file, the switch off and a missing table) passes together with the rules, text and motion specs. **Not verified:** migration 0046 on real Neon; the Variations page and the save in a real signed-in browser; a variation in real OBS (nobody has seen any of the alert studio there). **Known limits:** no sound preview on the page; the form has no text size or weight (a variation uses the standard ones); one variation per tip size; the animations and length from item 2 are the same for every tip (no per-size motion). ## Current state (2026-11-02, alert animations and timing built; alert studio item 2)

- **Alert animations and timing built (alert studio item 2; run migration 0045 BEFORE the deploy; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Alert animations and timing" in a creator's Paid features box, "Alert box" group; migration 0044 was run by the owner;).** A creator opens **Motion** in the dashboard (`/dashboard/motion`, nav link only with the switch on) and picks: how the card comes in (wipe, fade, slide up, zoom, bounce), how it leaves (wipe, fade, slide down, zoom out), how long it stays (normal, 5, 8, 12, 20 or 30 seconds) and a pause before the next alert (normal, 2, 5 or 10 seconds), with a live preview of the real card (landscape or vertical, a Play again button). **Rules** are in `src/lib/alert-motion.ts` (pure, shared by the page, the save action, the overlay and the tests): animations are keys from two short lists, the length is a whole number from 5 to 30, the pause from 0 to 10; anything else is refused when saving and dropped field by field at play time. **The card never leaves early:** a chosen length replaces only the usual "6 seconds plus reading time"; the sound, the voice and a WebM still hold the card up (`showMs` in `AlertStage.tsx`), and a creator rule's long stay still wins if longer. **Storage:** `users.alert_motion jsonb` (migration 0045), null is the normal card; `saveAlertMotion` (`src/app/actions/alert-motion.ts`) checks the switch and every field. **Overlay:** the alert JSON carries `motion` (`src/lib/alert-extras.ts`, read only while the switch is on at play time, in its own try so a missing column only drops the motion); both cards (alert and the follow-up message card) use it. Animations are CSS only: the card's `animation` is built from variables (`--in-name`, `--out-name` and their times) that `data-in` and `data-out` set, the keyframes use the separate `translate` and `scale` properties so they do not fight the card's own centring transform, and the reduced-motion rule still replaces the whole animation with a plain fade. The pause is a timestamp the play loop checks before taking the next alert from the queue. **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/alert-motion.spec.ts` (what may be picked, stored junk, the play-time switch, the loader against the real schema in memory including junk rows and a missing column) and two new browser tests in `test/overlay.spec.ts` (motion sets `data-in`, `data-out`, the animation names and `--dur`; no motion is the wipe) pass; the other overlay specs still pass in installed Chrome against the production build. **Not verified:** migration 0045 on real Neon; the Motion page and the save in a real signed-in browser; how each animation looks (the preview, a real browser, real OBS: nobody has seen it); the pause between two real queued alerts (the logic is a timestamp check, no browser test); bounce and slide use `translate` and `scale` properties, which need OBS's browser (Chromium 104 or newer, any current OBS). **Known limits:** no "delay before the message appears" (it would drift from the lit spoken words); length is chosen from fixed steps in the page, though the server accepts any whole number from 5 to 30; the length and pause apply to the message card too; one set of choices for every tip (per tip size comes with item 3). Owner to do: run 0045, tick the switch for a test creator, open Motion, try each animation in the preview, Save, send a test alert in real OBS, and say what to change. Items 3 and 4 followed the same day (see the entries above).

## Current state (2026-11-02, alert text and colours built; alert studio item 1)

- **Alert text and colours built (alert studio item 1; migration 0044 was run by the owner 2026-11-02; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Alert text and colours" in a creator's Paid features box, "Alert box" group; owner delegated the decisions: separate switches per item, a fan pick always beats an automatic variation, build order 1, 2, 3;).** A creator opens **Text** in the dashboard (`/dashboard/text`, nav link only with the switch on) and sets: the headline line (`{name} tipped {amount}`, plain text, up to 40 characters, `{name}` required once, `{amount}` optional and then the big amount line is not repeated), the card, text and amount colours (`#rrggbb` from a colour picker, each with a Reset), text size (smaller, normal, larger) and weight (regular, bold), with a live preview of the real card (landscape or vertical, three sample tips). **Rules** are in `src/lib/alert-text.ts` (pure, shared by the page, the save action, the overlay and the tests): no markup (the headline is rendered as text), only the two placeholders may use curly brackets, control and invisible characters are stripped, size and weight are keys from short lists, and colours must reach a contrast of 3 against the card they sit on (the creator's card colour, or the theme's: accent on Classic and Blocky, dark on Midnight and Neon, light on Paper); an unreadable pair is refused when saving and dropped again at play time (for example after the creator changes accent or theme), so a bad value never hides an alert. **Storage:** `users.alert_text jsonb` (migration 0044), null is the normal card; `saveAlertText` (`src/app/actions/alert-text.ts`) checks the switch and every field server-side. **Overlay:** the alert JSON carries `text` (`src/lib/alert-extras.ts`, read only while the switch is on at play time, its own try so a missing column only drops the text); both the alert card and the follow-up message card get colours, size and weight, the headline applies to the alert card only (the message card still says "Name says"); the lit-word highlight and the small tags follow the text colour. **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/alert-text.spec.ts` (27 tests: headline rules, colour rules per theme, form parsing, resolving a stored row field by field, the variables, the play-time switch, the loader against the real schema in memory including junk rows and a missing column) passes; the unit run is 609 passed (the 115 failures are the browser specs that need a running site, unchanged); `overlay.spec.ts` and `overlay-pinned.spec.ts` pass in installed Chrome against the production build (25 passed; the 2 failures are the known ones that expect no `DATABASE_URL`). **Not verified:** migration 0044 on real Neon; the Text page and the save in a real signed-in browser; the card with a custom headline and colours in real OBS or even a browser (nobody has seen it); Hindi names in the custom headline use the same font fallback as before. **Known limits:** one headline for the alert card; no per-amount text (that comes with automatic variations, item 3); the headline is not read aloud (only the message is); colours are free hex but readability is enforced. ## Current state (2026-11-02, rate limits shared and atomic)

- The per-network hourly limits for the contact form, the waitlist and starting a tip now share one helper, `src/lib/rate-limit.ts` (`clientIp`, `ipKey`, `takeAttempt`). It takes a per-network lock, so a burst of parallel requests can no longer all pass before any is counted (contact and waitlist had no lock; tips already did). A visitor with no reported address used to share one "unknown" count on contact and waitlist (5 and 20 an hour for everybody); that shared count now gets ten times the limit. Tips are unchanged on purpose: no address, no salt or a failing check still lets the fan pay. Same tables, no migration. The query was tried inside a rolled-back transaction (3 allowed, the 4th refused, nothing left behind); the forms themselves were not tried.

## Current state (2026-10-31, updated after the copy consistency pass)

- **UI lift on the tip page and the creator dashboard (2026-11-02; no migration, nothing to run;, tip page first).** The owner said both still looked too simple. **Tip page:** the alert preview is now a small stream frame holding the lime alert card ("<name> tipped", ink text, as DESIGN.md already described; it was a dark card before); the creator panel has a bigger avatar and name, a channel line only when it differs from the display name, and three icon rows instead of chips; the QR tile moved below the alert preview so the preview shows on a normal laptop screen; the steps have lime numbered badges instead of `01 / 03`; the amount sits in its own well with the chips in a 3-column grid. The perk rows say only what is true (UPI and cards, no account or email, an alert on their stream; no speed claim). **Dashboard:** every `Tile` title is now a semibold line with a lime icon badge (one lookup table by tile id in `parts/Tile.tsx`, so all dashboard pages change together), and the four overview numbers are larger with the estimated share under a rule. Files: `TipForm.tsx`, `AlertCard.tsx`, `CreatorPanel.tsx` (new `QrTile`), `Tile.tsx`, `OverviewStrip.tsx`, `DESIGN.md`. **Verified:** eslint, tsc and `next build` pass; looked at in real Chrome at 1440 and 390 px against a throwaway in-memory database (the live database was not touched). **Not verified:** the tip page with sounds, picks, pin and birthday sections switched on; other dashboard pages (Tips, Recap, Sounds, and the sub pages); the success screen; the dashboard on a phone; the Playwright suite (not run). Next: the owner looks and says what to change; the dashboard's text-heavy tiles (goal bars, alerts) could be shortened.
- **Tip page picks, voice minimum and dashboard tab scroll (2026-11-02; run migration 0043 BEFORE the deploy;).** (1) **Dashboard tabs:** a mouse wheel (or a touchpad scrolled up and down) over the tab row now moves it sideways (`useWheelScroll`, `src/components/ui/useWheelScroll.ts`; at either end the page scrolls as normal); the row no longer selects text. (2) **Alert rules are now picks on the tip page, not automatic by amount.** A rule (GIF, picture or video and/or a sound, at one fixed amount) shows as a tile in "Streamer's picks"; tapping it plays its sound, sets the amount and clears the message; the message box and the pin are switched off while it is picked; changing the amount or tapping it again drops the pick. A pick stands alone: the server (`checkRulePick` in `src/lib/alert-rules.ts`, run in `createTipOrder`) refuses a message, a soundboard sound or the voice with it, and an amount that is not the rule's. The tip stores `tip_orders.alert_rule_id`; the overlay applies a rule (media, sound, stay) only to a tip that picked it, so a soundboard or voice tip no longer gets a rule's media or stay. Only rules of ₹20 or more are offered (the site minimum). `resolveRule` (match by amount) is removed. (3) **Tip page sections:** "Streamer's choice or voice" (default or read aloud, off by default), "Soundboard" (a row to swipe sideways, or scroll with the mouse wheel, with a preview button on each sound; tapping the chosen one again clears it), "Streamer's picks" (same kind of row). A sound still excludes the voice. (4) **Owner's voice minimum:** `/admin/settings` ("Site settings") sets the smallest tip that can be read aloud for everyone, ₹50 until changed (table `site_settings`, `src/lib/site-settings.ts`); the creator's own amount can only be higher. Applied on the tip page, in `createTipOrder`, the overlay route and the speech route; the dashboard setting says so; each change is audited. Files: `db/migrations/0043_rule_picks_and_site_settings.sql`, `ExtraPicker.tsx`, `TipForm.tsx`, `creator.ts` (`getTipRules`), `alert-extras.ts`, `tip.ts`, `admin/settings/*`. **Verified:** tsc, eslint, `next build`, and all logic specs pass (new `test/tts-floor.spec.ts`, rewritten `test/alert-rules.spec.ts`); the browser specs cannot run on this Mac, same as before. **Not verified:** the tip page and the sideways rows in a real browser or on a phone, a real pick end to end in OBS, the admin settings page. Without 0043 a pick is refused with "not available" and the voice minimum stays at ₹50. Decided here (the owner may change any): picks need ₹20 or more; the pick's amount is fixed; the name field stays available with a pick.
- **Test voice read "the previous emoji" (2026-11-02, no migration, nothing to run).** The owner's friend swapped emoji in the dashboard's Test voice box and heard the same word. Not a stale clip: the server builds the spoken text fresh each time, but many look-alike emoji shared one spoken name (😂 🤣 😆 all "laughing"; 🎉 🎊, 🐶 🐕, 🌸 🌺 🌻 and about 50 more groups), and 36 emoji (the 24 clocks, 🔝 🔚 🔛 🔜 🔟 🏧 🪅) had no name and were silent. **Fix:** the first emoji of each group in `src/lib/emoji-names.ts` keeps the shared playful name (😂, 🐶, 🎉 and the other common ones are first); the others take their own official Unicode name, written into `src/lib/emoji-names-cldr.ts` as `CLDR_GROUPED_NAMES` by `npm run emoji:refresh` (the script now also does that; it ignores the `DISTINCT` list when working out which emoji are "ours"); a short hand-written `DISTINCT` list settles pairs whose official names were still the same (medals, 🐕/🐇/🐎, 🐱 🐈, the clocks and a few more). New `test/emoji-names.spec.ts` fails if two emoji ever share an English or a Hindi name, or if a renamed emoji loses a name. `test/message-filter.spec.ts` got one change (the official-name table may overlap `DISTINCT`). **Verified:** the full emoji scan shows 0 shared names (it was 56 Hindi groups plus 18 English pairs) and only the five skin-tone swatches silent; eslint, tsc and the two emoji unit specs pass. **Not verified:** how it sounds with the real voice (the owner should type 😂 then 🤣 then 😆 in Test voice); the Hindi I wrote for about 45 emoji (clocks, medals, animals, a few symbols; marked in the code) has not been read by a native speaker. **Known limits:** a long official name is read in full ("rolling on the floor laughing"), a message still names at most 3 emoji, and skin-tone swatches alone are still silent.

Signing in first and pressing Connect again showed the authorization screen. That is Streamlabs' flow and cannot be merged into one step from our side; `StreamlabsManager.tsx` now says so under the Connect button. Verified: eslint and tsc pass. - **Alert rules: "stay on screen" for big tips (2026-11-02; run migration 0042 BEFORE the deploy).** A rule from ₹1,000 up can keep the alert on screen for 30 s, 1, 2, 3 or 5 minutes (the database refuses a stay under ₹1,000 or over 300 s; the action checks the same). Option A, owner's pick: alerts show one at a time, so tips that come in meanwhile wait their turn (they stay queued up to an hour); the creator can press Skip to end it early. A WebM loops for the whole stay instead of stopping at 10 s. The stay only lengthens the normal time, never shortens it, and only applies while the rules switch is on at play time. A rule may now be a stay alone. (2) Vercel emailed that the free team's Function Storage (10 GB) was full: cause is one kept Production deployment per push (about 40 a day) and a 30-day retention; the fix and the safe delete command are in `OPERATIONS.md` section 10; the owner was setting Deployment Retention and deleting old deployments by hand, result not yet seen. (3) The owner asked to remove Vercel from the Mac only (not the account or the live site): nothing was deleted yet; what is left is the saved login in `~/Library/Application Support/com.vercel.cli` and the `vercel` package in `~/.npm/_npx`, plus a Vercel API token to delete in Account Settings > Tokens. - **Streamlabs bridge built (2026-11-02; run migration 0041 BEFORE the deploy; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Streamlabs alerts" per creator, "Alert box" group;).** A creator connects their own Streamlabs account on `/dashboard/streamlabs` (nav link only when the switch is on) and each paid tip also shows as an alert in their Streamlabs alert box (`POST /alerts`, `type=donation`: `*Name* tipped *₹250*`, the fan's message as the second line). **Permission asked:** `alerts.create` only. **Sign-in:** `/api/streamlabs/start` and `/callback` (random state in a cookie, checked on return; the code is swapped for tokens with the registered redirect `https://freetip.in/api/streamlabs/callback`); tokens are sealed with `YT_TOKEN_KEY` in `streamlabs_connections`, never logged or sent to the browser; an unknown token life is handled by refreshing once on a refused token. **Sending:** after capture the tip is queued in `streamlabs_outbox` (test-mode money, uncaptured tips, a switched-off feature and no connection queue nothing) and sent from the queue, inside the existing `after()` step so Razorpay is never held up; also right after the creator approves a message, and from `/api/cron/streamlabs-outbox` (daily safety net in `vercel.json`; an outside scheduler every 2 minutes is optional). Rules: at most about 2 alerts a minute per creator (31 s apart, Streamlabs' advice); the message follows the OBS alert's per-mode rule and is always dropped for a banned word; names are 2 to 25 characters (shorter is Anonymous), asterisks and control characters are stripped; an alert more than an hour old is dropped; five failed tries drops it and the tip still counts; a refused refresh pauses the connection ("needs you to sign in again") until the creator connects again. **Privacy:** new processor row and a retention paragraph on the privacy page; disconnect and account closing delete the sealed sign-in and the queue (it holds no fan text). **Also:** admin Health lists the two variables and the cron; `OPERATIONS.md` section 7c, the variables table, the cron table and the migration list are updated. **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/streamlabs.spec.ts` (30 tests against the real schema in memory with a pretend Streamlabs: the rules, queueing, sending, spacing, two workers at once, approval waiting, banned word, too late, token refresh and its failure, refusal, retries and the last try, test button, disconnect, account closing, sign-in) passes together with the paid-features, account-close, fan, pinned, retention, recap and admin specs; the signed-out redirects of the new page and routes and the cron's 401 pass against a real server. **Not verified:** a real Streamlabs sign-in and a real alert (the owner tests with the whitelisted account); that Streamlabs accepts the form-encoded body, shows the alert as intended and takes the rupee symbol; the page in a browser; the browser specs could not run on this Mac (no Playwright browser). **Known limits:** the Streamlabs app is in testing mode, so only whitelisted accounts can connect until Streamlabs approves it for full access (process undocumented); a creator with both the Freetip alert and a Streamlabs alert box in OBS sees each tip twice; no revoke call on disconnect (their docs list none). Plan and as-built notes: `docs/streamlabs-bridge-plan.md`. Owner to do: run 0041, set `STREAMLABS_CLIENT_ID` and `STREAMLABS_CLIENT_SECRET` in Vercel, redeploy, tick the switch for yourself, connect, press Send a test alert, and tell me what it looks like.
- **Streamlabs bridge researched, not built (2026-11-02; docs only, nothing to run).** `docs/streamlabs-bridge-research.md` has what the Streamlabs API allows (`POST /alerts`, `POST /donations`, OAuth 2, about 2 calls per minute per user as advice), the blocker (an app that is not approved can be authorized by only 10 whitelisted users; the approval process is not documented), what is not known (whether a donation pops the alert box, INR support, Streamlabs' API terms) and a design sketch (own switch, encrypted tokens, retry queue under the rate limit, privacy text first). Recommendation: build only if creators ask, and only after the owner registers a Streamlabs app and one real alert is proven. Nothing was tried with a real app or token.
- **Post-stream recap (2026-11-02; free for every creator, no switch, no migration, nothing to run).** New page `/dashboard/recap` (nav link "Recap" for every creator with a handle) lists the creator's latest five streams from the last 30 days: when it ran (IST), total tipped, tips, supporters, biggest tip, "you'd keep about" (same per-tip rounding as the tips list), top three supporters, and a "Copy summary" button. **What a stream is:** we never learn when a creator goes live, only when tips arrive, so a stream is a run of captured tips with no gap over 3 hours (`STREAM_GAP_MS` in `src/lib/stream-recap.ts`); nothing is stored, it is worked out on each page load from `tip_orders` (`loadRecapStreams` in `src/app/dashboard/queries.ts`, capped at 5,000 tips). A stream whose last tip is under 3 hours old says more tips may still join it. Same name in another case or spacing counts as one supporter; anonymous and blank names are counted apart and never a supporter. **Privacy:** the copied summary has numbers only (no fan names or messages), because fans agreed to be named on the stream alert, not in a post; there is no public or shareable recap page (the owner earlier chose not to publish fans' names). **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/stream-recap.spec.ts` (11 tests: grouping, the exact gap edge, order, rounding, name merging, anonymous, top three, ongoing, summary text) passes with the unit config; the signed-out redirect check in `test/routes.spec.ts` passes. **Not verified:** the page in a browser with a real signed-in session and real tips (nobody has looked at it); the browser-based specs could not run on this Mac because no Playwright browser is downloaded (`npx playwright install`). **Known limits:** a stream that has a long break (over 3 hours) is split in two; two streams on the same day with a short gap between them merge into one. Next free item from the competitor pass: Streamlabs bridge (check Streamlabs' current connection rules first). The remembered fan name, TEST badge and OBS-link warning were already built in phase 1 (the findings doc was out of date and is fixed).
- **START HERE (handoff rewritten 2026-11-01 at the end of the phase 4 session; chat cleared after this).** Last commit before phase 4: `e9ed58c`; phase 4 is committed on top of it (see `git log`). **Where the nine features stand:** built and OFF per creator (owner ticks them in `/admin/creators/<id>`, "Paid features"): custom alert media, alert rules, overlay themes, fan levels, fan list, birthday message, regional language voices, paid highlighted messages (phase 6, 2026-11-01; 8 of 9). **Phase 5, fan voice notes, was DROPPED by the owner on 2026-11-01 and is not to be built** (its old design stays in the plan file only for reference). Nothing planned is left unbuilt. Full plan: `docs/nine-features-plan.md`; next free migration number is **0041**. **Migrations 0037 and 0038 were run in Neon by the owner (2026-11-01, reported in chat; not checked against the database).** **Owner to do, in order:** (00) run migration **0040** (pinned messages) in Neon BEFORE deploying phase 6, then see the phase 6 entry below; (0) run migration **0039** (regional voices) in Neon BEFORE deploying phase 4, then see the phase 4 entry below; (1) [done] migrations 0037 and 0038; (2) deploy (the code is pushed, check Vercel built it); (3) call the daily cron once with the `CRON_SECRET` bearer (`/api/cron/tip-text-purge`) and expect `{"cleared":N,"contactDeleted":N,"timestampsDeleted":N,"fansDeleted":N}`; (4) tick fan levels, fan list and birthday message for one test creator, send a test tip with a name (and a birthday) and look at the alert in real OBS and at `/dashboard/fans`; (5) check `WAITLIST_SALT` is set in Vercel with at least 16 characters and never rotate it; (6) look at the new tip page, dashboard, admin and alerts in a real browser and say what to change. **Decisions taken this session (owner can change):** test-mode tips count towards fan levels (not the ledger); the birthday greeting is shown on the alert but not read aloud (the privacy text says "shown"); creators may keep a private note per fan (300 characters); no backfill when a feature is switched on later; a failed post-capture step for one tip is not retried (that tip is not counted). 
- **Admin: paid features can be switched on `/admin/features` itself (2026-11-01, no migration).** The page now has a search box (name, email or handle; a search ignores the "only creators with a feature on" filter) and a "Change a creator's features" list with the same tick boxes and Save as `/admin/creators/<id>` (`RowForm.tsx`, same `setCreatorFeatures` action and audit row). Checked: tsc, eslint, `npm run build`; not tried with a real signed-in session. A creator must have signed in once to exist.
- **Phase 6: paid highlighted messages, "pinned messages" in the code (2026-11-01; run migration 0040 BEFORE the deploy, after 0039; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Paid highlighted messages" in a creator's Paid features box, "Alert box" group;).** **What it is:** a fan who tips at least the creator's pin minimum can tick "Pin my message on stream for N seconds" on the tip page; once the creator approves that message it shows as a card on a new OBS source (alert link + `/pinned`) for the creator's chosen time. **Priced as a minimum tip, not an extra fee** (the fan pays exactly the tip; nothing in the fee rules, terms or fee pages changed). **Settings** (`/dashboard/pinned`, nav "Pinned", only with the switch on): smallest tip in whole rupees (never under the site minimum of ₹20; empty means not offered) and seconds (10 to 120, default 30), saved in `users.highlight_min_paise` and `users.highlight_seconds`; `tip_orders.highlight` and `tip_orders.highlight_until` (migration 0040). **Always approved first, in every alert mode:** a pinned tip in instant mode plays like split mode (`pickNext` treats it that way; the overlay route reads the flag only while the creator's switch is on at play time): name and amount play at once, the message follows only after the creator approves it; instant-mode pins appear in "Messages to approve" with a note (`readPending` includes only pinned ones in instant mode). The pin time starts at approval (`pinApprovedTip`, its own step with its own try, so a missing migration 0040 never undoes an approval), never at payment. **Owner-approved remove control (added to the plan the same day):** (1) "Take down now" on `/dashboard/pinned` and (2) "Take down pinned message" next to skip and replay in the alert tile (only with the switch on) both call `unpinAll`, which ends everything pinned now; the OBS card clears on its next check (3 seconds); (3) nothing brings a taken-down message back except the creator pressing "Pin again" on that message (`pinAgain`, within 24 hours of the tip, only for an approved message that asked for it); (4) a newer pin ends any older one at once, so an older one can never reappear behind it; (5) the admin switch is the last fallback (switching it off makes the overlay and the page go empty at once). **Money wording:** removal never refunds; the tip-page option says so ("approves it first and can take it down at any time. Tips are final, so if it is not pinned or is taken down, the tip is not refunded"), the community guidelines section "If a message is removed after you paid" now says "held, rejected or taken off the stream, by the creator at any time or by us for breaking these guidelines" (no banned words), and the refund page already said held, rejected, removed or not approved. **Server checks:** `createTipOrder` re-checks the switch, the minimum and that there is a message (`checkPinRequest`), and refuses with words for the fan before payment (a fan who asked for a pin never silently loses it); the overlay API `GET /api/overlay/<token>/pinned` answers `{pinned: null}` unless the switch is on and an approved, captured message is inside its time; banned words are checked on every call (a message the creator banned later shows nothing, a banned name shows as Anonymous); a refunded or charged-back tip stops showing. **Marketing guard** (`test/pinned-guard.spec.ts`): scans every file under `src/app` and `src/components` that is NOT in a private place (`app/[handle]`, `app/admin`, `app/dashboard`, `app/overlay`, `app/api`, `app/actions`, `app/editor`, `components/admin`, `components/overlay`, `components/tip`) plus `lib/legal.ts`, `lib/site.ts` and `lib/redirects.ts` for phrases such as "highlighted", "highlight your message", "paid highlight", "pinned message" and "pin ... message", after removing the stream-timestamps "highlights page", YouTube "pinned comments" and a CSS class; I proved it fails when a phrase is added to the about page. **Difference from the plan:** the plan said to grep "highlight" and "pinned" over all of `src/app` outside `[handle]` and `admin`; that would also hit the dashboard, overlay and API code that must use the words, and a bare "highlight" appears in glow-effect comments, so the dashboard is a private place and the phrases are specific. **Files:** `src/lib/pinned.ts` (rules), `src/lib/pinned-db.ts` (database half, no site imports so tests run it in memory), `src/app/actions/pinned.ts`, `src/app/actions/messages.ts` (approve starts the pin), `src/app/dashboard/pinned/`, `src/components/overlay/PinnedOverlay.tsx`, `src/app/overlay/[token]/pinned/page.tsx`, `src/app/api/overlay/[token]/pinned/route.ts`, tip page `TipForm.tsx`, `creator.ts` (`getPinOffer`), `SuccessScreen.tsx`, `alerts.ts`. **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/pinned.spec.ts` (24 tests: the offer, the settings form, the fan's request, the overlay resolver with banned words, instant mode still waiting for approval, the migration's checks, approve starts the time, a tip that did not ask or another creator's never pins, switch off, a newer message replaces the older, unpin, pin again within a day and not otherwise, refunded tips, banned words added later, the dashboard list) and `test/pinned-guard.spec.ts` (3) pass; the unit run is 524 passed; new browser spec `test/overlay-pinned.spec.ts` (7) plus the overlay, routes, metadata and tip-thanks browser specs pass on installed Chrome (51 passed). **Not verified:** migration 0040 on real Neon; the Pinned page, the tip-page option and the approve and unpin buttons with a real signed-in session and a real payment; the card in real OBS (nobody has seen it); the `make_interval` and boolean parameters under postgres.js against Neon (PGlite ran them). **Known failures that are not from this change:** the browser specs `overlay.spec.ts` "the API answers 503 without a database" and "the confirm route ... needs a database" fail when `.env.local` has a `DATABASE_URL` (they expect no database and get 404); 105 other specs in the unit config (routes, title screen, waitlist) need a browser and a running site and cannot run there. **Known limits:** one pinned message at a time per creator; the card has no animation out (it disappears when its time ends); the pin time is the creator's setting at the moment of approval; a fan who is anonymous can still pin (shown as Anonymous with the amount); "Pin again" works for 24 hours only; removing a message does not refund (tips are final). Owner to do: run 0040, tick the switch for a test creator, set a minimum, send a test tip with the pin ticked in each alert mode, approve it and look at the card in real OBS, press "Take down now" and check it clears, and say what to change. Next: nothing planned is unbuilt; the open list is the polish pass, the post-stream recap and the Streamlabs bridge.
- **Phase 4: regional language voices (2026-11-01; run migration 0039 BEFORE the deploy, after 0038; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Regional language voices" in a creator's Paid features box, "Alert box" group).** A creator with the switch on sees a **Regional language** picker under Voice in the dashboard settings (None, Tamil, Telugu, Kannada, Malayalam, Bengali, Gujarati or Marathi); it is saved in `users.tts_lang` (null by default). **What it does:** a message the fan typed with letters of that language's own script is read by that language's Azure voice (`src/lib/regional-voices.ts` holds the table, `prepareSpeech(message, regional)` in `tts-pure.ts` picks it); everything else is exactly as before (Hinglish and Devanagari as Hindi, other text as Indian English). Only the creator's one chosen language counts (a Telugu message to a Tamil creator is not read in Telugu). **Marathi and Hindi** share Devanagari: with Marathi chosen, Devanagari the fan *typed* is read as Marathi; Roman Hinglish (which the converter turns into Devanagari) is still Hindi. **Roman-letter messages ("Tanglish" and the like) are read by the English voice**, because nothing here can tell which language they are. Female and male voice follow the creator's existing Voice choice. **Voice names checked 2026-11-01 against Microsoft's Azure Speech language-support page** (all standard neural voices, one female and one male each, offered in every text-to-speech region including `centralindia`, which is ours): Tamil `ta-IN-PallaviNeural` / `ta-IN-ValluvarNeural`, Telugu `te-IN-ShrutiNeural` / `te-IN-MohanNeural`, Kannada `kn-IN-SapnaNeural` / `kn-IN-GaganNeural`, Malayalam `ml-IN-SobhanaNeural` / `ml-IN-MidhunNeural`, Bengali `bn-IN-TanishaaNeural` / `bn-IN-BashkarNeural`, Gujarati `gu-IN-DhwaniNeural` / `gu-IN-NiranjanNeural`, Marathi `mr-IN-AarohiNeural` / `mr-IN-ManoharNeural` (the existing Hindi and English voices are on the list too). `test/regional-voices.spec.ts` pins exactly these names. **Gates:** in synthesis (`creatorRegionalLanguage` in `tts.ts`, read at play time through `effectiveRegionalLang`, so the speech route and the dashboard test button both obey it: switch off, missing column or a bad stored value means today's behaviour) and in `saveSettings` (choosing a language needs the switch, checked on the server; clearing it is always allowed; a form without the picker leaves the saved language alone). **Polly has no regional voice:** with Azure down or not configured a regional message plays silently (the alert card still shows). Same 500,000-character monthly cap and per-creator daily cap as before (regional text counts per character like any other). **Also changed:** speech cleanup keeps a zero-width joiner or non-joiner that sits between two letters (it is part of Malayalam and Bengali spelling; it used to become a space and split the word; hidden characters elsewhere are still removed), and a message in a regional script skips emoji instead of reading their Hindi or English names in the middle of it. The dashboard "Test the voice" box gets a "Fill in a <language> sample" link for the creator's language. `test/foundation.spec.ts` now uses `voiceNotes` as its "not built" example. **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/regional-voices.spec.ts` (20 tests: the verified voice table, language choice, the Marathi and Hindi tie-break, the switch, joiners, emoji, banned words) and the 458 logic specs pass. **Not verified:** a real Azure call (no Azure key on the build machine, so none of the seven voices has been heard or even requested; the names rest on Microsoft's list); migration 0039 on real Neon; the picker and save in a browser with a real session; the seven sample sentences (written by us, a native speaker of each language should read them, and the owner should listen); how natural each voice sounds on slang. **Known limits:** one regional language per creator; Assamese uses the Bengali script and would be read as Bengali; no per-language env override for the voice names (only the Hindi and English female voices have one); Polly cannot take over. The privacy policy needs no change (same processor, same text, same retention). Next: phase 5, fan voice notes (always creator-approved, 30 days, private store, privacy text first, riskiest).
- **Phase 3: fan levels, fan list and birthday message (2026-11-01; run migration 0038 BEFORE the deploy, after 0037; all three OFF for everyone until the owner ticks them in a creator's Paid features box, under the new "Fans" group).** **Privacy policy first (owner's rule):** the policy now says what a fan profile is (name typed, a hashed fan code, tip count, total, points, level, first and latest tip, optional birthday day and month, the creator's private note), a new section "Fan profiles: what a creator sees and a stream shows", a purposes row, 12-month retention (after the latest tip; creator can delete a fan; closing an account deletes all; the birthday on a tip goes with the name at 90 days) and the "Remember my name on this device" browser storage that shipped in phase 1 and had never been disclosed. **Fan identity** (`src/lib/fan-profiles.ts`): `fan_key` = HMAC-SHA256 with `WAITLIST_SALT` over creator id and the normalised name (NFKC, trimmed, one space, lower case), so the same name is one fan per creator and cannot be linked across creators; a profile is made after capture (`afterCapture`, own try, never blocks the books) only when the creator has fan levels or the fan list on at that moment and the tip has a name (anonymous, blank and unconfirmed tips never count); `fan_xp_events` (key = tip order id) makes a repeated webhook count once; a whole payment taken back (refund or lost chargeback, in the webhook, for any payment including test-mode) removes the points and drops an empty, note-less profile. **Test-mode tips count too** (unlike the ledger) so the owner can see levels with Razorpay test keys. Missing `WAITLIST_SALT` (under 16 characters) means nothing is counted (logged). **Levels** (`src/lib/fan-levels.ts`): 1 point per whole rupee; level N starts at 50·N·(N−1) points (2 at 100, 3 at 300, 4 at 600, 5 at 1,000), cap 50; the alert shows "Lv N" and "Level up! Lv N" on the tip that raised it (read at play time from `fan_xp_events` joined to the profile; switch off at play time drops it; not on the follow-up message card; a name the creator banned later shows no level). **Fan list** (`/dashboard/fans`, nav "Fans" for creators with any of the three on): search by name (wildcards literal), five sorts, up to 200 shown (all in the download), private note (300 characters), delete a fan (profile, counted tips and the birthday on them), CSV at `/api/dashboard/fans/export` (formulas defused with `csvCell`, times in IST), all gated on the server with `hasFeature`. **Birthday message** (`src/lib/birthday.ts`): the creator writes a greeting (120 characters, `{name}` = fan's name) on the Fans page; only then does the tip page ask for an optional birthday (month and day selects, shown only for a named, non-anonymous tip); `createTipOrder` re-checks the switch and the greeting, validates a real day (29 Feb allowed) and silently drops it otherwise, stored as `tip_orders.birthday_md` ("MM-DD"); the alert shows "🎂 greeting" only when that day equals today in India time and the finished line passes the creator's banned words; it is **shown, not read aloud** (no speech change); the profile keeps the latest declared birthday for the fan list. **Retention:** `purgeOldFanProfiles` (12 months after `last_at`, plus its counted tips, audit row `fans.purged`) and the tip birthday clear run in the daily `tip-text-purge` cron (reply now has `fansDeleted`; each in its own try so a missing 0038 stops nothing else); `closeCreatorAccount` calls `eraseFans` (profiles, counted tips, birthdays on the creator's tips, the greeting). **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/fan-identity.spec.ts` (28 tests against the real schema in memory: levels, keys, counting, repeats, anonymous and switched-off tips, reversal, delete, 12-month purge, 90-day birthday clear, account closing, list search and sort and CSV, birthday rules and India-time day, alert resolver and loader with the switch turned off after the fact) and a new browser spec in `test/overlay.spec.ts`; the unit suite passes and the browser specs for overlay, metadata, routes and interactions pass on installed Chrome (56); screenshots of the card with level and birthday at 1920×1080 and 1080×1920 looked right. **Not verified:** migration 0038 on real Neon; the tip page birthday selects, the Fans page, the save, note and delete actions and the CSV download in a browser or with a real session; the levels and greeting in real OBS; `WAITLIST_SALT` present in production (the rate limiter already needs it). **Known limits (also what the policy says):** a profile is only a name (same name merges, anyone can type another's name, a new spelling is a new fan); switching a feature on later has no history (no backfill; names older than 90 days are gone anyway); a failed `afterCapture` step is not retried by a sweep, so that one tip is not counted; the birthday is self-declared and unchecked; the dashboard TEST alert does not show a level or birthday. Next: phase 4, regional voices (check every Azure voice name first).
- **Phase 2 step 4: overlay themes and fonts (2026-11-01; run migration 0037 BEFORE the deploy; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Overlay themes and fonts" in a creator's Paid features box).** A creator picks a card style and a font at `/dashboard/style` (nav "Style", page reachable only with the switch on) with a live preview (sample tip of ₹99, ₹500 or ₹2,000, landscape or vertical, in the creator's own accent colour); "Save style" calls `saveOverlayStyle` (`src/app/actions/overlay-style.ts`), which re-checks the switch and that both choices are keys from the fixed lists. **Fixed lists in code, no uploads:** `src/lib/overlay-themes.ts`, themes Classic (today's card, the default), Midnight (dark card, accent on amount and spoken words), Paper (light card with an accent bar), Neon (dark card, accent edge and glow), Blocky (square corners, thick dark edge, hard shadow); fonts Freetip (default), Poppins, Bangers, Pixel (Press Start 2P, set smaller), Playfair, self-hosted by `next/font` in `src/components/overlay/fonts.ts` (CSS variables `--font-ov-*`, put on the wrapper of the overlay page and of the preview). The font applies to the name and message only; the amount stays in the mono number style so it always fits. The creator's accent still colours every theme. **Storage:** `users.overlay_theme` and `users.overlay_font` (migration `0037_overlay_themes.sql`, text with defaults `classic` and `default`; the plan's number 0040 was not used, later features take the next free number). **Playing:** `loadAlertExtras` reads them only while the switch is on at play time (own `safely` lookup, so a missing 0037 only drops the style); `resolveAlertExtras` returns `theme: {theme, font}` or null (switch off, never picked, or both defaults give the plain card); an unknown stored key plays as the default, so a theme can be retired from the code; the overlay API sends it as `alert.theme`, so a change shows on the next alert with no OBS reload; both the alert card and the follow-up message card wear it. `AlertStage` maps it to `--c-*` CSS variables on the card (`overlayStyleVars`); the wipe animation's clip-path now extends 3% of the width beyond the card so a glow or hard shadow is not cut off (nothing changes for a card without one). **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass (the fonts download at build); new `test/overlay-themes.spec.ts` (the lists, unknown keys, variable safety, the switch at play time, the loader against in-memory Postgres including a retired key and a missing column) and two new browser specs in `test/overlay.spec.ts`; the full browser suite on installed Chrome (desktop) is 551 passed, 2 skipped (and the expected-failure `@x` waitlist specs); screenshots of all five themes with their fonts at 1920×1080 and 1080×1920 looked right. **Not verified:** the Style page and the save against real Neon (no database here), the mobile Chrome and iPhone projects, and the cards in real OBS. **Known limits:** a dashboard TEST alert does not wear the style (it is made up in the overlay, which does not know it); the four fonts have no Hindi letters, so Hindi names and messages fall back to the plain font (the Style page says so); Midnight and Blocky have a very dark edge, which is faint over a dark game scene. Next: fan levels, fan list and birthday message (phase 3; the privacy policy text comes first).
- **Phase 2 step 3: alert rules by tip size (2026-11-01; migration 0036 already run, nothing new to migrate; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Alert rules by tip size" in a creator's Paid features box).** A creator sets up to 10 rules at `/dashboard/rules` (nav "Rules", page reachable only with the switch on): "from ₹N up, show this media and/or play this sound" (whole rupees, 0 to 1,00,000; saving a size that already has a rule replaces it; a rule must have media or sound). `saveRule` / `deleteRule` (`src/app/actions/alert-rules.ts`) re-check the switch, that the media and sound belong to the creator, and the 10-rule cap; delete is always allowed. **Playing:** `src/lib/alert-rules.ts` `resolveRule` picks the rule with the highest size at or below the tip; `resolveAlertExtras` applies it at play time: the fan's own sound pick beats the rule's sound, which beats the creator's default; a rule's media plays instead of the rotation, and if that file is not playable (off, held, deleted) the tip falls back to the normal rotation; a voice tip keeps the rule's media but no sound; the follow-up message card never gets a rule; rules off means default sound and plain rotation; media off means a rule's sound still plays. Media rules need "Custom alert media" on; sound-only rules work without it. **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/alert-rules.spec.ts` (rule choice, precedence, the switches, the loader against in-memory Postgres) and the 436 logic specs pass; `test/foundation.spec.ts` now uses regionalVoices as its "not built" example. **Not verified:** the rules page and actions against real Neon, and the browser overlay specs (need a server; none of this touches `AlertStage`). **Known limit:** a dashboard TEST alert ignores rules. Next: overlay themes.
- **Phase 2 step 2: custom alert media (2026-11-01; migration 0036 already run, nothing new to migrate; OFF for everyone until the owner ticks "Custom alert media" in a creator's Paid features box).** Creators upload GIF, PNG, JPG, WebP or WebM files that play in the OBS alert. **Creator side:** `/dashboard/media` (nav link "Media", shown and the page reachable only when the switch is on; redirects to `/dashboard` otherwise): choose several files, the browser sniffs the first bytes (`checkMediaFile`), measures a picture's size or a WebM's length (over 10 s is refused), sends the file straight to Blob (`/api/media/upload` issues a token for `media/<creator id>/` only, image and video types only, 8 MB, and re-checks the switch and the 20-file cap), then `addMedia` fetches the file again, re-checks its bytes, size and the 20 files and 40 MB allowance (files taken down do not count) and saves the row. Each file has a name, a **placement** (above the card, behind its text at 30% opacity, or across the whole stage behind the card), a "Play it on my alerts" switch and Delete; a taken-down file shows as such and cannot be switched back on. **Approval:** a creator's files are held (`review_state` new) while the creator has no approved file, so a second upload cannot skip the first check; `/admin/media` (nav "Alert media") lists waiting files first with a preview, **Approve** and **Take down** (erases the file, keeps the row as a note, both write `media.approved` / `media.removed` to the audit log). **Playing:** `loadAlertExtras` reads the creator's approved, enabled files only when the switch is on at play time and `pickMedia` picks one by the tip's id (same tip, same file; several files take turns; alert rules by tip size, next, will let the creator choose instead); the alert card gets it, the follow-up message card never does, and a voice tip loses only its sound. `AlertStage` preloads it before the card (image decode or video `canplaythrough`, 6 s limit; any failure drops the media and the plain card plays), never plays sound (a video is muted), stops a WebM at the 10 s cap whatever the file says, extends the card to a WebM's length, and a file that errors while showing just disappears; the stage was extended with `.ov-stack` (above), `.ov-media-behind` and `.ov-media-fullscreen`. Account closing erases the rows and deletes the files (`eraseMedia`). **Verified:** tsc, eslint and `npm run build` pass; new `test/alert-media.spec.ts` (pickMedia rules, the switch at play time, the loader against in-memory Postgres, helpers, account closing) and `test/foundation.spec.ts`, 421 logic specs pass; the 17 overlay specs, including four new media ones (above, behind, fullscreen, a file that fails to load) and the other 67 browser specs that run without a database pass on the installed Chrome (the bundled Chromium could not be downloaded here); screenshots of all three placements at 1080p and vertical looked right. **Not verified:** the upload, the save and the admin buttons against real Blob and Neon (no database or token here), a real WebM, and the alert in real OBS (transparent WebM and GIF behave as in Chrome, but nobody has seen it in OBS). **Known limits:** a dashboard TEST alert shows no media yet; no automatic scan for adult content, so the owner's first-upload approval is the only check; copyright is the creator's responsibility (the dashboard says so). Next: alert rules by tip size, then overlay themes.

- **Stream timestamps, phase 3: the OBS clip helper (2026-11-01; run migration 0034 BEFORE the deploy, after 0033).** Real trimmed clips from the creator's OWN OBS recording, uploaded to their Discord. **Server:** `db/migrations/0034_clip_helper.sql` (`users.ts_helper_token_hash`, own token apart from the chat command and editor link, and `ts_helper_seen_at`); `src/lib/ts-helper.ts` (token lookup, newest id, `loadHelperEvents`: moments after an id, oldest first, 20 per poll, with each moment's `ageSeconds` by the DATABASE clock so no clock on the creator's PC has to match ours, `seconds` into the stream, label, source; no viewer names; `replayBufferNeeded(delay)` = delay + 45); `GET /api/ts-helper/[token]/events?since=<id>` (long poll up to 20 s, no `since` means "tell me where to start", 404 for any bad token, 400 for a bad `since`, 503 with no database); `generateHelperToken` / `removeHelperToken` and a "Video clips from your own recording" section on `/dashboard/timestamps` (token shown once, helper seen in the last 90 s, replay buffer length to set); account closing clears the token; `planFeatures(...).clipHelper` gates it. **Helper app:** `tools/obs-clip-helper/` (own `package.json`, plain ESM Node, no build step, excluded from eslint and tsc; `ffmpeg-static` bundled). `main.mjs` polls Freetip, queues moments (one at a time, no repeats, max 20), waits until a moment is 12 s old, saves OBS's replay buffer over OBS's WebSocket server (`obs.mjs`, own small obs-websocket 5 client with the password login), measures the file, `trim.mjs` works out the 20 s before and 10 s after, `ffmpeg.mjs` re-encodes to fit `maxUploadMb` (default 10), `discord.mjs` uploads from the creator's PC with a caption and a link to that second. The webhook address lives only in the helper's `config.json`. README and launchers (`start-windows.bat`, `start-mac.command`) are in the folder.
  - **Verified:** tsc, eslint, `npm run build` pass; 19 server tests in `test/ts-helper.spec.ts` (token rules, ordering, `since`, own creator only, ages including the delay, seconds into the stream, page size, no viewer names, seen-at throttle) plus the extended closing test in `test/retention.spec.ts`; 26 helper tests (`cd tools/obs-clip-helper && npm test`: trim maths, queue, config check, feed reader, OBS login string against the protocol's published example, Discord upload, and a run through a PRETEND OBS WebSocket server with a REAL ffmpeg cut: a 50 s video cut to a 30 s clip at the right place, and a 2 MB limit honoured). The built site answers the feed route with 503 without a database and 400 for a bad `since`.

(2) **Editor link:** `/editor/<secret>` (own hashed token, shown once, regenerate cancels the old) lists the creator's latest streams, most-marked moments first, with `Download CSV` (`/editor/<secret>/csv?video=...`, quotes cells and defuses `=+-@` formulas) and `Copy as YouTube chapters` (`buildChapters`: first at 0:00, at least 3, 10 seconds apart; says why when it cannot). Settings for all three are on `/dashboard/timestamps`. Account closing also removes the editor link and turns public pages off; the privacy page says what these show. - **Stream timestamps, phase 1 (2026-11-01, owner request, idea from the open-source Tsnip; run migration 0032 BEFORE the deploy).** Viewers or mods type `!ts` (or `!ts Nice shot`) in YouTube live chat through Nightbot (or StreamElements, Fossabot); Freetip saves the moment and replies in chat (`Saved 12:34 Nice shot`); when the stream ends one comment with all the times is posted from the creator's OWN channel; each moment can also go to the creator's Discord (a webhook they paste; one message with stream title, who, what, time and a link that opens the stream at that second; it is a link to the moment, not a video file, because YouTube has no way to cut a clip). Our own code, not copied. (phase 2: auto-marks for big tips and goals, public highlights page, editor link and exports; phase 3: an OBS helper that saves and trims real clips from the creator's own recording).
  - **Pieces.** `db/migrations/0032_stream_timestamps.sql` (users columns, `timestamp_streams`, `stream_timestamps`, `ts_rate`); pure rules in `src/lib/timestamps.ts`; chat command `GET /api/ts/[token]` (always answers 200 with a sentence); `src/lib/stream-timestamps.ts` (`recordMoment`, `postDueComments`, behind an injected YouTube client); `src/lib/youtube.ts`; `src/lib/discord-webhook.ts` (only Discord's own webhook address is accepted, nothing in a message can ping anyone); `src/lib/secret-box.ts` (AES-256-GCM, key `YT_TOKEN_KEY`); `src/lib/plans.ts` (`planFeatures`, everything on for everyone: one place to gate a paid plan later, no price or plan shown anywhere); cron `/api/cron/stream-timestamps` (safe to call every few minutes); opt-in connect flow `/api/yt-connect/start` and `/callback` (a separate Google consent for `youtube.force-ssl`, NOT part of sign-in; refuses a Google account that is not the creator's own channel); creator page `/dashboard/timestamps` (Connect YouTube, the Nightbot command shown once, delay 22/42/58, Discord, recent streams) with a new nav link.
  - **Rules.** One comment per stream, claimed before posting so two runs cannot double-post; an unclear YouTube answer is never retried; an expired Google permission asks the creator to reconnect; YouTube's daily limit leaves it queued; streams not the creator's, made for kids, comments off or never live are skipped with a reason shown. Per command: 30 a minute, one per viewer per 5 seconds, 500 per stream. Moments are counted from YouTube's real start time minus the creator's delay. A blocked word (built in or the creator's own) is dropped from the title, the moment is still saved.
  - **Data.** Viewer chat names and what they typed are deleted after 90 days (`purgeOldTimestamps`, inside the daily `tip-text-purge` cron); closing an account erases the moments and the YouTube and Discord secrets and cancels the YouTube permission with Google. Privacy page and processors list updated (the old "we never post to YouTube" and "we keep no refresh token" lines were wrong for this feature and are rewritten as optional); `LEGAL_UPDATED` is 1 November 2026.

Until Google verifies the app, creators must reconnect about every 7 days.

- **Handle rename built (2026-11-01, audit gap 4; owner's rules: a creator may change the handle once, ever; the old link and QR code stop working at once (404); the old handle stays reserved for that creator for good).** Run **migration 0030** (`handle_history`: old handle as primary key, unique per creator) BEFORE the deploy: without it claiming or changing a handle fails. Rules are in `src/lib/handle.ts` (`claimHandle`, `renameHandle`, `checkHandle`, the reserved-name list moved there, `overlay` added): both run in one transaction under the same advisory lock, so a name cannot be claimed while it is being given up; the unique index on `handle_history.user_id` makes "once" hold under concurrency; a failed attempt (taken, same name) does not use up the change; closed accounts keep their handle and cannot rename. No field or action changed. Removed `focus:outline-none` from the dashboard inputs (settings, goals, payout, OBS link, voice test) so the global lime focus ring shows, as already done on the tip page. tsc, lint and build pass; **not seen in a browser**. Handle rename (audit gap 4) is still open and waits for the owner's answers on old-handle redirect, rename limit and reservation.
- **Test voice "engine did not answer" and QR PNG fixes (2026-11-01).** (1) QR: "Download PNG" said "Could not make the PNG" because the inline SVG has no `xmlns`, so the browser could not load it as an image (the SVG download was a broken file too). `CreatorQR.tsx` now serialises it with `XMLSerializer` and the namespace. (2) Voice: once the earlier error was fixed the real message was Azure not answering. Likely cause (not confirmed, no Vercel log seen): `AZURE_SPEECH_REGION` written as "Central India" (the portal's display name) or a key with a trailing space; both returned silently. `tts-azure.ts` now trims the key, turns the region into its code (`centralindia`), and logs a clear `TTS:` line, including Azure's status and reply text. **Owner:** set `AZURE_SPEECH_REGION=centralindia` (or whatever the code is for your resource), redeploy, retry; if it still fails send the `TTS:` line from the `/dashboard` function log. tsc, lint and build pass; not seen in a browser.
- **Test voice error fixed (2026-11-01, third pass).** The Vercel log showed the real cause: `Error: A "use server" file can only export async functions, found object` on `/dashboard`. `src/app/actions/creator.ts` exported four plain constants (`HANDLE_IDLE`, `PAYOUT_IDLE`, `SETTINGS_IDLE`, `CLOSE_ACCOUNT_IDLE`), which Next.js refuses; they now live in `src/lib/creator-state.ts` and the four dashboard forms import them from there. The same log showed `relation "goals" does not exist`: **migration 0028 has not been run in Neon yet** (the goal bar cannot load until it is). tsc, eslint and build pass; not tried on the live site.
- **Several goals, timezones and carry-over built (2026-11-01, run migration 0028 BEFORE the deploy).** A creator can have up to 3 goal bars (`MAX_GOALS` in `src/lib/goal.ts`); they stack on the same `/goal` OBS source. Goals moved from the single set of columns on `users` to a new `goals` table (migration `0028_multi_goals.sql`; it copies each existing goal once, guarded by `users.goals_migrated` so a re-run cannot bring back a removed goal; the old `users.goal_*` columns are left in place and no longer read). Each goal has a **timezone** (a list of 29 zones in `GOAL_TIMEZONES`, India the default; the day, Monday-start week and month are cut at that zone's midnight, daylight saving handled in `periodStart`) and a **carry over** switch: when a repeating goal falls short, the shortfall is added to the next period's target, never more than one extra target (`carryPaise`), chained through the finished periods in `goal_history`. The bar is measured against the set target plus the carry and the overlay says "includes ₹X carried over". `goal_history` now belongs to a goal (`goal_id`, nulled if the goal is removed so the past stays), stores the carry and the zone, and the old one-row-per-creator-and-kind primary key is dropped. New `src/lib/goal-progress.ts` (`loadGoalProgress`: what every bar shows, writes a missing finished period itself if the daily job has not) is used by the overlay route (now returns `goals`, plus `goal` for an older open OBS page) and the dashboard; `closeFinishedPeriods` works per goal and takes a clock. The dashboard tile has a form per goal, an add form while under 3, per-goal remove (`removeGoal`; `clearGoal` is gone). Account closing now erases the goals and their history. New `test/goals-multi.spec.ts` (6 tests against the real schema in memory, including the migration copy and re-run) and new unit tests in `test/goal.spec.ts` pass; tsc, eslint and the build pass. **Not checked:** the browser test for stacked bars (`goal.spec.ts`, needs Chromium, blocked here), the dashboard forms in a browser, the SQL against real Neon, a real OBS source. **Known limits:** carry applies only to repeating goals; a goal edited mid-period changes the target used for the period's history row; the history list is the last 8 rows across all goals; timezone list is fixed (no search of every zone).
- **Repeating goal bar (built 2026-11-01, run migration 0027 BEFORE the deploy).** A creator can set the goal to repeat daily, weekly (Monday start) or monthly, in India time. The bar counts tips since the start of the current period (`periodStart` in `src/lib/goal.ts`), so it resets by itself with no job and nothing stored. Finished periods go to `goal_history` (target, raised, reached or missed), written by the new daily cron `/api/cron/goal-periods` (00:10 India) and when the creator opens the dashboard, once per period. The dashboard shows this period's progress and the last 8 periods; the overlay title adds "· this week". (Other timezones, several goals and carry-over were built the same day, see the entry above.) Checked: tsc, eslint, build, period-maths unit tests; the browser tests for the bar need Playwright's browser, which is blocked here, and the SQL has not been run against a database.
- **Test voice error, second pass (2026-11-01).** Cause still not reproduced (no session or Azure key locally). Two changes: the dashboard route now has `maxDuration = 30` (Azure's 6 s plus Polly, which has no timeout, could outlast the default function time and show as a failed call), and the whole server action is wrapped so any throw is logged as `Voice test: unexpected failure.` and shown as a message. **If it still fails:** open the Vercel function log for `/dashboard` right after pressing the button and send me the line that starts with `Voice test:` or `TTS:`; also try from a phone or a personal browser, since the work laptop's web filter may block the request.
- **Owner's phone test fixes (2026-11-01).** Google sign-in works on the owner's phone; on the work laptop it did nothing because the employer's web filter blocks one of the site's JavaScript chunks ("PageRisk"), so the page never hydrates. Not a site bug and not fixable in code; sign in from a phone or a personal browser. Fixes: (1) the **Dashboard button** now shows in the top bar on phones when signed in (it sat inside a `hidden sm:flex` wrapper; Sign out stays in the menu on phones). (2) **Test voice** no longer pre-fills the box or offers sample chips; the box starts empty with a placeholder (`TEST_SAMPLES` stays in `src/lib/voice-test.ts` for the unit tests). (3) **Test voice page error:** cause not reproduced (needs a signed-in session and the Vercel log); the server action now catches a throw from the speech engine and the button catches a failed call, so it shows a message instead of an error page. If it still happens, read the Vercel function log around the click. (4) **Sign-in redirect:** `/signin` and `/signup` now send an already signed-in visitor to `/dashboard` instead of showing Google again. Not tried with a real login. tsc, eslint, build and 734 browser tests (installed Chrome; Playwright's Chromium and WebKit downloads are blocked on this network) pass.
- **Payout destination wording made consistent.** The terms (definition of Payout, "Who can use Freetip") and the "Get paid" step now say a creator's UPI ID or bank account, matching what the dashboard form collects and what the rest of the terms and the FAQ already said. Nothing was changed about KYC itself: the wording is now "identity and account checks".

The pricing page's search and share description now names both charges. **The combined cost to a creator is 7.75% (5% plus 2.75%), not 7.5% as an older note in docs/next-steps.md said**; ₹100 leaves ₹92.25. The headline number and the heading "One fee" (no longer in the code) are the owner's marketing call.
- **Hindi copy (`docs/hindi-copy.md`).** Two rows said a 2.5% processing charge; fixed to 2.75% in Devanagari and Roman script. The "Get paid" row now says UPI ID or bank account. Still stale and not changed: a row for an older FAQ payout answer (it says "bank account" and "timing will be published at launch"). A native Hindi speaker should still read the changed rows.

Cashfree Easy Split publishes no such threshold (0.2% per split on top of gateway fees, vendors settled directly, KYC per vendor) but is run by an aggregator that the same RBI rules cover, so it may apply there too.
