## Current state (2026-10-31, updated after the rules check)

- **No lawyer or accountant (owner has no budget), so the rules were read from public sources: `docs/rules-check.md`.** Main finding: collecting fan payments in our account and paying creators about 92% of each looks like "aggregation of payments to merchants and settling to them" under the RBI Payment Aggregator Directions (15 Sep 2025, cl. 4(i), 4(f), 10(a), 16(a)); a licensed aggregator needs ₹15 crore net worth. So the manual payout sheet (step 4) is the risky design and is **not to be built**. Cleaner models: a licensed aggregator splits and settles to creators (Razorpay Route if eligible; Cashfree Easy Split lists creator platforms), or creators collect into their own merchant account. GST and TDS/TCS are summarised with what stays unclear (GST registration below ₹20 lakh for inter-state services is disputed between sources; whether tips count as a "sale of services" for 194-O and s.52 is unanswered). This is a reading, not legal advice. **Owner next:** send the Razorpay email (`docs/razorpay-email-draft.md`); the Cashfree email is drafted in `docs/cashfree-email-draft.md` (send both; their answers choose the payout model). No code changed.

## Current state (2026-10-31, updated after payout step 3: admin balances)

- **New owner page `/admin/finance/balances` (payout engine step 3, admin half; read-only, moves no money).** Per creator: balance (creator_payable on the ledger, can be negative), held (tips still inside their waiting period), available (balance less held, never below zero), next release date, and a status (Ready, Below minimum, No UPI ID, Creator pending or suspended, Owes us, first payout). Hold rules are one pure module, `src/lib/balances.ts`: 7 days for a UPI tip, 30 for any other method or an unrecorded one, 60 for those while the creator has no completed payout; minimum payout ₹500. Held is worked out per tip from the ledger's own postings for that tip, so a tip Razorpay took back nets to zero and is not held. Query in `src/lib/admin/balance-queries.ts`; nav link "Balances". No migration. Tests: `test/balances.spec.ts` (9: the pure rules, and the query against the real schema in PGlite including a reversed tip, a completed payout and the status rules); tsc, eslint and the production build pass.
- **Not done on purpose:** the creator `/dashboard/payouts` page. The design says creators see "available" only once the legal answers are in, so the dashboard keeps showing an estimate. The ₹500 and hold numbers are the defaults from `docs/payout-design.md` section 3, still the owner's to change; they are constants in `balances.ts`, not environment settings yet. **Not verified:** the page in a browser (no Chromium here), and against a real Neon database. The 96 browser specs fail here with and without this change (no Chromium); CI runs them.

## Current state (2026-10-31, updated after the tiered message length)

- **Message length now grows with the tip (idea taken from HyperChat's tiers, owner picked 50% / 75% / 100%).** The creator's "message limit" is now the longest message, for tips of ₹300 and up; ₹100 to ₹299 gets about three quarters, under ₹100 half, each rounded to the nearest 10 and never below 30 (or the ceiling, if lower). The default 150 gives 80, 110 and 150. The rule is one pure function (`src/lib/message-limit.ts`: `messageLimitFor`, `nextMessageStep`), used by `createTipOrder` (the server rejects a longer message and says which tip size allows more) and by the tip form (the counter follows the amount, a hint says what a bigger tip allows, and a message that is too long for a lowered amount shows a note and keeps Pay off instead of being cut silently; the textarea still stops at the creator's full limit). The dashboard slider is relabelled "Longest message" with a line explaining the steps. No migration: it reuses `message_max_chars`, so existing creators keep their number as the ceiling. Public copy ("message limit") stays true. Tests: `test/message-limit.spec.ts` (5, pure); tsc, eslint and the production build pass. **Not verified:** the tip form in a browser (no Chromium here, CI will run the existing specs, and none of them exercises this), and a real tip rejected by the server. Competitor notes from this pass (HyperChat, StreamTipz, SuperTip policy pages, prepaid tokens, what not to copy) are in the chat only; the findings file `docs/competitor-findings.md` was not updated.

## Current state (2026-10-31, updated after the SuperTip-style policy pass)

- **Policy pages aligned with SuperTip's set, in our own wording (owner request; the owner pasted SuperTip's pages because their site blocks automated reading).** New **`/community-guidelines`** (who it applies to, respect, never allowed, honest money, privacy and impersonation, no spam, extra rules for creators, how we enforce, a message removed after payment stays a final tip, reporting, changes), with its own share image, linked from the footer, sitemap (once `LEGAL_DRAFT` is false), terms, refunds and related-page links. **Terms:** guidelines made part of the terms; "not live yet" line removed; "anything our payment partner or the banks do not allow"; third-party links; no endorsement of creators; new sections "Mistakes on our site", "Ideas and feedback" and "General" (severability, no waiver, whole agreement, what is owed survives closing). **Refunds:** a blocked name or message is stopped before payment so it is never charged; new "Money back from a creator" (we can't refund for a creator); the suspension threat on chargebacks softened to "we may block further tips from you". **Delivery:** renamed "Shipping and delivery policy" (path stays `/delivery`), no tracking numbers, no email receipt from us, new "The records we keep" (payment reference, payment time, alert time). **About and pricing:** "one plain fee" replaced, because creators pay 5% plus 2.75% ("What creators pay", "Simple pricing", share image "Simple pricing."); About now says creators can sign up. **Privacy:** "(after launch)" removed; creator data now matches the onboarding form (Google name, email, picture, handle, channel; legal name and UPI ID; no phone number); Azure Speech added as the voice processor with Polly as fallback; Neon role updated; sale or merger clause; PCI-DSS line; "planned" processors read "(when we switch it on)". **Draft banner** now says only "Draft. Details in [brackets] are still to be filled in, and the text is waiting for legal review." `LEGAL_UPDATED` is 31 October 2026.
- **Not copied from SuperTip, on purpose:** "you may not dispute with your bank" and the permanent-ban threat (a bank's dispute right cannot be waived by our terms; a lawyer should confirm), "blocked messages are not refunded" (ours never charges them), "direct settlement" and "zero holding periods" (we hold and pay on a schedule), "age of majority" (owner dropped the 18+ rule on 2026-10-25), a thinner privacy policy, Nagpur jurisdiction.
- **Verified:** tsc, eslint and the production build pass; `fees-copy` and `ledger` unit tests pass (37); every policy page and the new share image answer 200 with one h1 on a local production server. The new route is added to `routes`, `responsive` and `interactions` specs, but the browser tests could not run here (no Chromium); CI will run them. **Still needs a lawyer**, and the operator details in `src/lib/legal.ts`.

- **Account closing built (2026-11-01, owner approved; the privacy policy already promised it).** A creator closes their own account from **Close your account** at the bottom of the dashboard (types CLOSE; outline buttons, no lime), and the owner can close one from `/admin/creators` (Close account, then Confirm close) when a creator asks through the contact form. Rules are in `src/lib/account-close.ts`: the users row is **emptied in place, never deleted** (tips, ledger, payouts and the audit log point at it and must be kept): name, email, picture, YouTube channel, bio, display name, banned words, goal, OBS link, default sound and accent are erased, the sounds are deleted (files removed after the database change; a failure leaves orphans for the daily clean-up job), the account is set to suspended, `google_id` becomes `closed:<id>` and the email `closed-<id>@closed.invalid`, so the same Google account can sign up again as a **new** account. The **handle stays reserved** on the old row (so nobody takes over an old QR code); the tip page returns 404 for a closed account. `legal_name` and `upi_vpa` are kept **only if money was ever posted for that creator** (they are then part of a payout record; **ask the accountant**), otherwise erased. **It refuses (and changes nothing)** when the ledger balance is not zero, a payout is scheduled, held, initiated or in transit, or a captured live tip is not yet in the books; the creator is told to use the contact form, and the owner sees the reason on the admin page. Every close writes an `audit_events` row (`account.closed`, no personal data). The owner can no longer reset a closed account to pending. The privacy policy now says closing erases the profile straight away and the handle stays reserved. `test/account-close.spec.ts` (7 tests, real schema in memory) passes; tsc, eslint and the build pass; **not seen in a browser, and the signOut redirect after closing is untested against a real session**. **Not done / to know:** closing a creator with a balance needs a person (the payout engine is not built); the privacy policy says tip names and messages are kept 90 days, **but no job deletes them yet** (a gap between the policy and the code); fans' data on a closed creator's old tips is untouched; contact-form messages are not erased by closing; the shared 'Last updated' date on the legal pages was not changed.

- **Tip-page accent colour built (2026-11-01, owner approved: free for everyone now; deeper customization is for a paid plan later, nothing about a plan shown on the site).** Migration `0026_creator_accent.sql` adds `users.accent` (null is the default lime) and `users.plan` (default 'free', unused, a slot for later). `src/lib/accent.ts` holds the rules: 10 presets, each tested to read on the dark page and with its ink (WCAG 4.5:1); the ink on the fill flips between dark and light; stored values that are not lower-case hex or not readable fall back to lime. ## Current state (2026-10-31, updated after "no refunds, record only")

- **Owner decision: tips are final, no refund or dispute feature** (the site wording already said so). Payout step 2 is cut down to recording only. A bank-forced chargeback cannot be stopped by a policy, so when Razorpay reports a whole payment taken back (`refund.processed` or `payment.dispute.lost`, handled in the webhook after the response, `src/lib/reversal.ts`), the capture posting is reversed line for line (creator share, our fee and the processing charge go back to `processor_clearing`), and the tip is marked `refunded` or `disputed`. Nothing is held, reserved or paid, a dispute is recorded only when lost, a partial amount is logged for the owner and not posted, and a tip never posted (test mode) is left alone. One reversal per capture, so repeats post once. A creator's balance can go negative; nothing blocks payouts yet because no payout code exists. Razorpay's own non-refundable fee is not recorded. Tests: 3 new in `test/ledger.spec.ts` (33 pass); tsc and eslint clean. **Not verified:** the real payload shape of Razorpay's refund and dispute events (the code reads `payload.refund.entity` and `payload.dispute.entity` with `payment_id` and `amount`), and that the webhook is subscribed to these two events in the Razorpay dashboard (**owner action: tick `refund.processed` and `payment.dispute.lost`**).

## Current state (2026-10-30, updated after payout engine step 1: the books)

- **Owner decisions today:** the payout design (`docs/payout-design.md`, v2) is approved for steps 1 to 3; the payout sheet (step 4) stays blocked until Razorpay, a lawyer and an accountant answer section 7 of that file. The processing charge is now a **flat 2.5%**, Freetip's own, on top of the 5% fee (a ₹100 tip leaves the creator ₹92.50, was ₹92.64). The design was attacked by a fresh reader before any code (it found a webhook coupling, a dead event store, a test-mode leak, zero-amount ledger lines, wrong build order, false copy) and the finished step 1 was attacked again (it found live tips that could drop out of the books, a double-encoded jsonb log, the webhook waiting on database work, misleading labels); all of it is fixed.
- **Built (step 1, the books):** `src/lib/money.ts` (the money rules in pure code: `splitTip`, `capturePosting`, `feeLines`, half-up to the paise, `toPaise`), `db/migrations/0023_ledger_accrual.sql` (live-mode flag and Razorpay's captured amount, currency, fee and tax on `payment_attempts`; `fee_lines.source` and a unique key; one reversal per posting; **one capture posting per tip**; an index on the Razorpay order id; a **commit-time check that every ledger transaction balances per currency and has entries**; creator_payable entries must name the creator; all safe to re-run), `src/lib/accrual.ts` (posts a captured live tip: debit `processor_clearing` the tip, credit `creator_payable` the creator share, `platform_fee_revenue` our fee, `gateway_fee_expense` the 2.5% charge; keyed `capture:<payment id>`; refuses test-mode, uncaptured, amount or currency mismatch; a daily sweep posts any missed), `src/lib/capture-follow-up.ts` (after the webhook's capture commits: saves Razorpay's captured facts, posts, logs a minimal event with no payer details; never throws; the webhook runs it in `after()` so it can never delay or undo a capture), `src/lib/razorpay-sweep.ts` and `src/lib/books-sync.ts` (asks Razorpay for the last 3 days of live payments, lists any the site never recorded, fills in a missing live-mode flag or captured amount from Razorpay's own figures, then posts), daily cron `/api/cron/accrual-sweep` (04:29 UTC), `livemode` saved in the same insert as the payment attempt (with a fallback while the migration has not run), admin finance pages rewritten to read the ledger and to say "could not read" instead of showing zeros (`src/lib/admin/*`, `src/app/admin/**`), a **Sync with Razorpay and post missing** button in `/admin/reconciliation` (a POST, owner only).
- **Test-mode money is never posted** (the ledger cannot be edited): only payments made with an `rzp_live_` key count. Existing test tips stay out of the books and out of the admin figures (shown as a count). Balances start at zero when real keys go live.
- **Site copy** (agent, checked): the flat 2.5% charge is worded as Freetip's processing charge everywhere (pricing, terms, delivery, FAQ, fee tile, dashboard note, five docs). The 30-day fee notice now covers the processing charge. The FAQ about large tips says a possible UPI merchant fee has been announced and nothing extra is added today. `LEGAL_UPDATED` is 30 October 2026. Public pages still promise no payout day, schedule or minimum.
- **Owner actions:** (1) run `db/migrations/0023_ledger_accrual.sql` in Neon **before** the deploy goes live (the code works before it too: statements fail, are logged, and capture and alerts carry on); (2) when real Razorpay keys go live, press Sync once and read `/admin/reconciliation`; (3) put the questions in section 7 of `docs/payout-design.md` to Razorpay support, a lawyer and a chartered accountant (drafts can be written); (4) check the Hindi and Hinglish rows in `docs/hindi-copy.md`; (5) decide the marketing question: the home headline says 5% while creators pay 7.5% combined, and `pricing/page.tsx` has the heading "One fee"; (6) ask the accountant whether GST applies to the 2.5% charge (the pages still say GST is charged on our fee where it applies).
- **Verified:** eslint, tsc and the production build pass; 224 unit-style tests pass (new: `money`, `ledger` (29, in-memory Postgres with the real schema and every migration run twice), `ledger-driver` (the real postgres.js driver against the in-memory database over the Postgres wire protocol: bigint as text, undefined-column code, jsonb object vs string, failure at commit, pool health), `books-sync`, `razorpay-sweep`, `admin-finance`, `fees-copy`). The in-memory helper refuses a JSON string bound to jsonb, which is the mistake the real driver would punish. Added dev dependencies `@electric-sql/pglite` and `@electric-sql/pglite-socket` (lockfile only npmjs) and `playwright.unit.config.ts` (`npx playwright test -c playwright.unit.config.ts test/money.spec.ts`: no build, no server).
- **Not verified, needs a real database or real keys:** lock and wait behaviour when the webhook and the sweep post the same payment at the same instant (relies on the unique key plus `on conflict do nothing`); the Neon pooler with commit-time trigger errors; that Razorpay sends `X-Razorpay-Event-Id` (the event log is simply skipped without it) and fills `fee` and `tax` at capture (the posting does not depend on either); the live Razorpay check; real owner login on the admin pages; how the Neon SQL editor runs the migration (one transaction or not).
- **Known limits:** the flat 2.5% is Freetip's margin or loss against Razorpay's real fee, not a pass-through; Razorpay's own charge, settlements, refunds, disputes and payouts are not recorded yet (the admin pages say so); a payment captured late under a different payment id than the attempt holds is only reported by the Razorpay check; the audit helper (who did what) arrives with step 4; "available" balances are not shown to creators until step 2 exists.

## Current state (2026-10-30, updated after voice auto-learning)

- 2026-10-30: owner parked auto-learning (cost): it stays off (no `TTS_AUTOLEARN`, no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`). Word logging stays on and is free; the owner will ask me to add words to the dictionary by hand. Cheaper option if revisited: switch the model in `tts-learn-run.ts` to `claude-haiku-5-5` (about 40 times cheaper).

- Automatic dictionary learning (owner request: no human step needed): daily cron `/api/cron/tts-learn` (04:13 UTC, `vercel.json`) takes the logged unknown words seen 3 or more times (up to 40 a run), asks a language model (`claude-opus-5-5`, structured output with Zod, `src/lib/tts-learn-run.ts`) whether each is ordinary Hindi, then **our own rules decide** (`decide` in `src/lib/tts-learn.ts`): the model must be confident, say not offensive and not also English; the word must be 4 to 20 plain letters, the answer pure Devanagari, nothing on the profanity lists in either script, and no rude root. Accepted words go to `tts_learned_words` (migration 0022, run it, it also adds `outcome` to `tts_unknown_words`) and the converter loads them every 10 minutes per server instance (`setLearnedWords`, `loadLearnedWords`). They are weak words: they convert only inside a Hindi clause and never override a hand-written word. English, rejected and learned words are marked with an outcome so they are not asked again. Only single words are sent to the model, never messages. `/admin/tts-words` now also lists learned words with Remove (a removed word is never relearned). Off until `TTS_AUTOLEARN=true` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` are set in Vercel; a failed or refused call changes nothing. No server-side refusal fallback was added: a refusal just means that batch is asked again tomorrow. Added `@anthropic-ai/sdk` and `zod` (lockfile still only npmjs). Tests: 2 new, `test/hinglish.spec.ts` 23 of 23, tsc, lint and build pass. **Not checked: a real model call (no key here), the SQL against Neon, and how good the learned words sound.** First run: set the two variables, run migration 0022, trigger the cron once, read `/admin/tts-words`.

## Current state (2026-10-30, updated after the Test voice button)

- **Test voice button built** (dashboard tile "Test the voice", shown only when the owner's `TTS_ENABLED` switch is on and the creator is not suspended). A creator types a message (or picks a Hinglish or English sample), presses the button and hears it through the real engine with no payment. It also shows the text the voice was given (Roman Hindi already turned into Devanagari, so the dictionary can be checked by ear and by eye), the voice name, which engine made the clip (Azure or Polly) and whether it came from the cache. This is the free way to do the pending real Hinglish voice test.
- Protection against spending the Azure free quota (`src/app/actions/voice-test.ts`, rules in `src/lib/voice-test.ts`): signed-in creators only; the text goes through the same cleanup and word filter as a fan's message (built-in profanity list plus the creator's banned words, checked on what was typed); a repeat of the same text is a free cache hit; a 6-second cooldown per creator (migration `0020_voice_test.sql`, column `users.tts_test_at`) because the free Azure tier answers 20 requests a minute for the whole site; and tests may use only half of a creator's daily characters (`TEST_SHARE`), so testing can never leave their real alerts silent. Tests count in the same usage table as real alerts, so the site-wide monthly cap covers them too.
- If the engine does not answer, the owner (signed in with an `OWNER_GOOGLE_IDS` account) gets a hint to check `AZURE_SPEECH_KEY`, `AZURE_SPEECH_REGION` and the `TTS:` log line; other creators get a plain "try again shortly".
- The TTS layer gained `synthesizeDetailed` (clip plus engine, voice, cached or not, or the reason there is no clip: off, cap reached, engine failed); `synthesize` is now a thin wrapper, so the alert path behaves as before.
- **Owner action:** run `db/migrations/0020_voice_test.sql` in Neon. Without it the button still works, only without the cooldown (the daily cap still holds). Then open the dashboard, press **Test voice** on the Hinglish sample, listen, and tell me how it sounds.
- Verified: tsc, eslint (0 errors) and the production build pass; 144 unit-style tests pass (5 new in `test/voice-test.spec.ts`: samples pass the filter and pick the right voice, refused input, blocked words, cleanup and length, the half-share cap). **Not checked:** the button in a real signed-in session, a real Azure call, the cooldown SQL against a real database, and browser autoplay on iOS Safari (the player is always shown so the creator can press play).
- Known limits: a creator can only type up to their own message limit (300 at most); the free tier's 20 requests a minute is shared by the whole site, so a burst of real tips plus tests can still hit it (the alert then plays silently).

## Earlier state (2026-10-30, updated after the Hinglish dictionary upgrade)

- **Hinglish dictionary made more advanced** (owner request, 2026-10-30). Found by running about 50 realistic Roman-Hindi tips through the converter: many common words were left in Roman letters (aata, dekhta, gaao, lagte, milte, kuchh, kahin, tabhi, khud), and one was wrong ("jitna", "as much as", came out as "जीतना", "to win", because the auto-respelling of "jeetna" caught it). What changed:
  - **Verb forms made from stems** (`src/lib/hinglish-verbs.ts`, new): about 80 stems (dekh, bol, khel, bata, khaa, aa, ja, de, le...) times a table of endings (na, ta, ti, te, o, unga, ega, enge, iye, kar, ke...) give about 3,000 forms with correct Devanagari. They are a new **weak** kind of word: converted only inside a clause that is already Hindi because of other words, and they never make a message look Hindi. So "bhai tu nacho" converts while "nacho and a banana for the stream", "I walked a mile" and "Jake and Karen say hi" stay English. Irregular verbs (kar, ja, aa, de, le, ho, kah, pi, ro) take only the regular endings from the table; their other forms are typed in `WEAK_EXTRA` (`hinglish-words.ts`).
  - **About 300 more hand-typed words** (`MORE` in `hinglish-words.ts`): the as-much-as family (jitna, utna), where/when words (kahin, tabhi, jahan), amounts and order (poora, saara, pehla, dusra), time, family, feelings, money, wishes, faith, exclamations, number words to ninety, and small words (sa, si, pi, har, jo, dum, khud, thak). None are common English words; names that are English words (Josh, Karen, Jane, Jake, Doge) are kept out of the always-convert list.
  - **Stretched spellings**: "bakwaas", "yaarr", "kyaa", "bhaaii", "bahutt" are matched by cutting back a held vowel or a held last letter until the word is a known one. A doubled consonant inside a word is never cut, so "challenge" is never read as "chalenge" (found and fixed during the dictionary audit). Four English words that would collapse by accident (boll, bool, dill, tariff) are blocked.
  - **Two grammar touches**: "the" after a Hindi verb is read "थे" ("rahe the", but "mujhe the best" is left alone), and "ki" is "कि" (that) after "bola", "hai", "tha" and similar, "की" (of) elsewhere.
  - **Bug fixes**: a fan typing "constructor", "toString" or "valueOf" could have found an inherited object property in the old lookup and had function text spoken; the lookups are now Maps. The respelling can no longer turn a rude word into a polite one ("chhod" never becomes "chod").
- Audited against the system English dictionary (310,000 words): every word in the always-convert table that is also English was reviewed (the rest are harmless Hindi or Sanskrit words such as agar or shiv), the stretched-spelling rule was checked against the whole dictionary (the 1 strong and 4 weak accidental matches left are rare words such as "yarr" and "beech"), and every made form that is an English word is weak so it only converts in a Hindi clause. A test now checks that every Devanagari value in all tables is well formed (no stray vowel signs).
- Verified: tsc, eslint (0 errors; one old unused-variable warning in `date-range.ts`) and the production build pass; the 13 unit-style specs (141 tests, including 13 new Hinglish tests) pass; the 2 `goal.spec.ts` tests need the dev server and were not run in this pass. **Not checked:** how the Hindi voice actually sounds with the new forms (needs the real Azure voice test), and messages in styles the 50-message test set did not cover.
- Known limits: "is" meaning "इस" ("is baar") is still left as English; names are not converted; a Roman word the tables do not know is still read by the Hindi voice as English letters; new slang still has to be added by hand (`MORE` for always-Hindi words, a stem in `hinglish-verbs.ts` for a verb).

## Earlier state (2026-10-30, updated after the Hinglish voice work)

- **Hinglish voice fix and a new engine layer built** (fifth item of the AcePressure queue). Polly could not be used (AWS free-plan accounts block it), so voice now has two engines: **Azure Speech** (REST, no SDK, free F0 tier of 500,000 characters a month; `src/lib/tts-azure.ts`) as the main one and Polly (`tts-polly.ts`) as the fallback if Azure is not set up or fails. `src/lib/tts.ts` is the layer: prepare the message, look in the cache, check the caps, try Azure then Polly, store the clip. Nothing changes for creators or fans; the owner's `TTS_ENABLED` switch still rules.
- **Hinglish converter** (`src/lib/hinglish.ts`, tables in `hinglish-words.ts`): ported from AcePressure's clause-aware method (a clause is Hindi when its Hindi words outnumber its English ones; words that are also English such as main, mat, to, do, ram only convert inside Hindi and not beside "the", game words, possessives and so on). Improvements over AcePressure: about 120 more everyday and stream words (verb forms, feelings, time, money, numbers, festivals; none that are common English words), and **respelling**: each word of 4+ letters also matches common respellings (aa/a, ee/ii/i, oo/u, w/v, z/j, chh/ch, a/i endings), about 450 generated spellings, never overriding a listed word and never catching an English word or common name (a blocklist plus a check against the system English dictionary). A message that looks Hindi is converted to Devanagari and read by the Hindi voice (`hi-IN-SwaraNeural`), anything else by the Indian English voice (`en-IN-NeerjaNeural`); the Polly fallback gets the same converted text.
- **Cache and caps** (migration `0019_tts_cache_usage.sql`): clips are cached by engine, voice and text so a replay or repeat costs nothing (`tts_cache`); characters sent are counted per day, creator and engine (`tts_usage`). Defaults: 3,000 characters per creator per day and 400,000 per month site-wide (under Azure's 500,000), overridable with `TTS_DAILY_CHARS_PER_CREATOR` and `TTS_MONTHLY_CHARS`. Over a cap the alert plays silently. A daily cron (`/api/cron/tts-cache-purge`) removes clips unused for 30 days. Before the migration voice still works, without cache or caps.
- **Status 2026-10-30 (owner, away from the PC):** the Azure Speech resource (Free F0) is created and `AZURE_SPEECH_KEY`, `AZURE_SPEECH_REGION` and `TTS_ENABLED` are added in Vercel. **Still to do, in this order:** redeploy in Vercel, run migration 0019 in Neon, set the Azure budget alert, then test a real Hinglish tip (free options: curl the Azure endpoint directly; or Razorpay test mode if the keys start with `rzp_test_`; or a ₹1 to ₹10 tip) and report how it sounds. Idea not built: a dashboard "Test voice" button that speaks a sample through the real engine with no payment.
- **Owner actions:** (1) run `db/migrations/0019_tts_cache_usage.sql` in Neon; (2) create an Azure Speech resource on the **Free F0** tier (region near India, for example Central India) and add `AZURE_SPEECH_KEY` and `AZURE_SPEECH_REGION` in Vercel; optional `AZURE_SPEECH_VOICE_HI` and `AZURE_SPEECH_VOICE_EN`; (3) set a budget alert in Azure; (4) keep `TTS_ENABLED=true` and test a real Hinglish tip in OBS. The Polly variables are now optional.
- Verified: tsc, eslint (0 errors) and the production build pass; desktop suite 223 passed, 2 skipped (new `test/hinglish.spec.ts` 7 tests and `test/tts-engine.spec.ts` 6 tests: conversion, English left alone, respellings, no English clashes, voice choice, SSML escaping, cache key, caps). **Not checked:** a real Azure call, the SQL against a real database, how the voices actually sound, what Azure does past the free quota (not confirmed whether it throttles or bills).
- Known limits: the free tier allows 20 requests a minute and the 500,000 characters are shared by all creators; the word list is still hand-made, so new slang needs adding (ideas: send unknown words to a model, a creator-editable pronunciation list); a Roman word the list does not know is read by the Hindi voice as English letters.

## Current state (2026-10-30, updated after the alert controls)

- **Test, skip and replay controls built** (third item of the AcePressure queue). Three buttons under "Alerts on stream" on the dashboard (`OverlayTile.tsx`, actions in `src/app/actions/overlay-controls.ts`, signed-in creator only, need an alert link). **Send test alert** shows a made-up card ("Test alert", ₹49) in OBS: no payment, no tip row, no voice, never confirmed to the server. **Skip current alert** clears the card on screen and drops any alert waiting behind it (a waiting one is confirmed as handled, otherwise it would come back when its 60 s lease ran out). **Replay last alert** makes the most recent shown alert (captured in the last hour, the overlay's own window) available again by clearing its shown and lease columns; a 10 s cooldown stops a double click replaying two, and it says so when there is nothing to replay.
- How it works: the buttons write timestamps (`users.overlay_test_at`, `overlay_skip_at`, `overlay_replay_at`, migration `0017_overlay_controls.sql`); the overlay's 4 s poll returns `controls: { skipAt, testAt }` with every answer and an overlay only acts on a time newer than the first one it saw, so an OBS reload never replays an old test or skip, and two open overlays both react. No client clock is compared. A test or skip takes up to 4 s to show; a test has a 3 s cooldown.
- **Owner action:** run `db/migrations/0017_overlay_controls.sql` in Neon **before or right after the deploy**: the overlay's main query reads the new columns, so the overlay fails until it exists (same pattern as 0015).

Known limit: replay plays before any newer unshown tips (it is ordered by the original tip time).

## Current state (2026-10-30, updated after reliable alert delivery)

- **Alert delivery is now lease and confirm** (the first item of the AcePressure queue). Before, the overlay API marked an alert shown the moment it handed it out, so an OBS reload between the hand-out and the card appearing lost that alert. Now `GET /api/overlay/[token]` only leases the alert for 60 seconds (`tip_orders.alert_lease_until`, `message_lease_until`, migration `0015_alert_leases.sql`), and the overlay page confirms through the new `POST /api/overlay/[token]/ack` once the card is on screen; only that sets `alert_shown_at` / `message_shown_at`. A lease that is never confirmed runs out and the alert is handed out again, so an alert can show twice but is not lost. Two open overlays still don't both play it (the lease blocks the second). The confirm checks that the token belongs to the tip's creator, and settles a message that rode along with the alert only on the first confirm, so a repeated confirm can't mark a message shown early.
- The overlay page (`AlertStage.tsx`) now holds at most one alert in its queue plus the one on screen, so leases can't run out while alerts wait; it retries a failed confirm once. The speech route accepts a leased message as "just handed out", because the voice is downloaded before the card (and so before the confirm).
- **Owner action:** run `db/migrations/0015_alert_leases.sql` in Neon **before or right after the deploy**: the overlay queries read the new columns and the overlay fails until it exists (the same pattern as migration 0010).
- Verified: tsc, eslint (0 errors; one old unused-variable warning in `date-range.ts`) and the production build pass; `test/overlay.spec.ts` and `test/overlay-rules.spec.ts` 52 passed in installed Google Chrome (desktop and phone), with new tests for the confirm after the card shows, no confirm from the preview, and the confirm route's bad-token, bad-body and no-database answers. Playwright's own browsers could not be downloaded here, so the run used a temporary config outside the repo. **Not checked:** the new SQL against a real database (no test Neon branch was used), and a real OBS reload. Please test: send a tip, reload the OBS source while the card is up (it must not replay) and just as it appears (it may replay once).
- Next in the AcePressure queue: (2) message cleanup before speech plus the banned-word and profanity filter, (3) test, skip and replay controls, (4) goal bar overlay, (5) Hinglish voice fix.

## Current state (2026-10-29, updated after the soundboard)

- **Soundboard built** (owner request, reversing the earlier "no soundboard on the tip page" choice). Ideas from the owner's AcePressure project (read, rules re-written, nothing copied; its eight starter sounds are deliberately **not** included). Creators upload their own sounds; the owner can remove any from `/admin/sounds`.
- How it works: `/dashboard/sounds` (new "Sounds" tab). Each sound has a name, emoji, minimum tip (₹0 to ₹10,000), volume (10 to 100), a "fans can pick it" switch, and the creator can mark one as the default that plays when a fan picks nothing (none = silent, as before). Limits: 30 seconds, 8 MB per picked file, 30 sounds and 60 MB per creator. Fans see a picker in step 02 of the tip page (first tile "Streamer's default", each sound with its price and a preview button; a sound above the tip amount is locked and blocks the Tip button with a reason). The server re-checks the pick in `createTipOrder` (sound belongs to the creator, is switched on, tip reaches the minimum). The OBS overlay plays the sound with the alert card, then the voice follows it; the card stays up for both. A paid pick still plays if the creator later hides or reprices it; deleting a sound clears the pick.
- **Auto-repair on upload** (AcePressure used FFmpeg; Vercel cannot run it): `src/lib/audio-repair.ts` decodes the file in the browser, refuses anything over 30 s, silent or unreadable (with a reason), resamples to at most 44.1 kHz mono or stereo and re-saves a standard 16-bit WAV. So MP3, M4A, OGG and WAVs with odd headers all end up the same clean format. The browser then uploads straight to Vercel Blob (`/api/sounds/upload` issues a token limited to WAV, 8 MB and the creator's own folder; a function would cap the body at 4.5 MB). `addSound` fetches the file back and checks the WAV header again, so duration and size come from the file, never from the page.
- Rules and tests: `src/lib/sounds.ts` (pure) and `test/sounds.spec.ts` (17 tests). The repair step was also run in real Chrome on six files: a 48 kHz stereo WAV with an extra chunk (repaired and accepted by the server check), a clean WAV (kept as is), 31 s, silent, random bytes named .mp3, and empty (all refused with reasons).
- Terms: new paragraph in "Your content and ours" (creators are responsible for their sounds, we may remove any); the Vercel row in the privacy processors list now mentions the sound files. Still draft, a lawyer should review.
- **Owner actions before it works:** (1) run `db/migrations/0013_creator_sounds.sql` in Neon; (2) in Vercel create a Blob store (it must be a public one, because OBS and fans' phones play the files directly) and make sure `BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN` is set on the project, then redeploy; (3) test: upload an MP3 on `/dashboard/sounds`, pick it on a tip page, play the alert in real OBS. Until the migration is run the site keeps working: the tip page shows no picker, tips without a sound save as before, and the overlay stays silent (each lookup fails soft). Until the token is set the Sounds page says uploads are not switched on.
- Verified: eslint (0 errors), tsc and the production build pass; desktop Playwright suite 184 passed, 2 skipped (installed Chrome via a config outside the repo; the 7 "x" marks are the existing `test.fail()` cases). Not checked: a real upload to Blob, a sound in real OBS, the picker on a real phone, WebKit and Safari (decoding there is the likeliest to differ), the lock and unlock when the amount changes (read through, not clicked). Known limits: a file uploaded but never saved leaves an orphan in Blob (no cleanup job yet); no fan-facing sound search or categories (not needed at 30 sounds).
- Next, one at a time (owner, 2026-10-29): (1) reliable alert delivery (lease and confirm, so an OBS reload does not lose an alert), (2) message cleanup before speech and the banned-word and profanity filter (not found in the code), (3) test alert, skip and replay controls, (4) goal bar overlay, (5) Hinglish voice fix (Roman Hindi to Devanagari), kept last; the TTS engine itself stays Polly for now.

## Current state (2026-10-29, updated after the logo seam fix)

- Fixed two hairline seams in the finished logo (owner report: a visible line on the F and the p). Cause: each letter is a separate clip of one traced shape, and clips that only touch draw their edge pixels at partial coverage. On the F the cut between F and r runs through the F's top arm (it overhangs the r), leaving a vertical line near the arm's end; the p had the same kind of line where its left cut meets the swoosh. Now neighbouring letter clips overlap by `SEAM` (2 logo units), and the F/r cut steps: above `ARM_BOTTOM` (234) the arm belongs to the F, below it the r starts at the cut (`src/components/logo/Logo.tsx`).
- Verified: zoomed screenshots (3x) of the F and p before and after in real Chrome show the lines gone at rest; tsc, eslint and the production build pass. Not run: the Playwright suite, and a frame-by-frame look at the letter swing (the overlap is 2 units, so it should not show). Check on a real phone.

## Current state (2026-10-29, updated after the logo performance check)

- **Decision: the logo is not changed.** The two ideas deferred earlier (pre-splitting the letter paths so the body path is not drawn as 8 clipped copies, and a precomputed spine point table) were measured first and are not worth doing. Setup: production build, real Chrome, Pixel-sized viewport (390x844 at 2x, touch), CPU throttled 4x and 6x to stand in for a low-end phone, 3 runs per case, frame times from `requestAnimationFrame` over 8 to 9 s, plus a CPU profile of the main thread. Two scenarios: the title-screen intro, and the large looping footer logo scrolled into view.
- Results: at 4x both scenarios hold about 55 to 59 fps, with a few 33 ms frames and no frame over 50 ms except one 100 ms frame at the start of the intro (page load, not the logo). At 6x they drop to about 45 to 52 fps. Hiding the logo with `visibility: hidden` (which removes all its painting, keeps its script) makes no consistent difference: footer 4x was 59/59/56 fps with it and 58/58/59 without; footer 6x was 40/55/50 with it and 45/44/47 without (the without runs were not better). Run-to-run noise is larger than any logo effect.
- Where the time goes (CPU profile, 4x, 8 s): `fillText` is about 31 to 34 percent of all CPU time, "(program)" (browser work such as compositing and painting) another 33 to 40 percent, the page's own script about 5 percent, and `getPointAtLength` (the logo's spine lookup) 0.5 percent (38 ms of 8 s). So a spine table would save under half a percent and the letter split nothing that shows.
- **The real hotspot, not touched here:** `fillText` comes from the canvas drawing of the "₹" coins in `src/components/GlobalBackground.tsx` (lines 37 and 57) and the same code in `src/components/sections/MoneyOrbit.tsx` (lines 38 and 58), which draw a text glyph for every coin on every frame. Rendering each coin face once to an offscreen canvas and stamping it with `drawImage` would very likely cut most of that, but it changes how the background is drawn, so it needs its own step, a before-and-after frame check and the owner's go-ahead. Not measured: a real phone (the owner's check), and iOS Safari.
- Method scripts live outside the repo (`/tmp/perf`) and are not kept; the numbers above are the record. No code changed in this step.

## Current state (2026-10-29, updated after the scroll-lock fix)

- **Found while starting the "page invisible until first scroll" step, and live on the deployed site until this commit:** the title-screen CSS in `src/app/globals.css` (`body:not(.page-scrolled) { overflow: hidden }` plus the content-hiding and animation-pause rules) was not limited to the home page. Nothing adds `page-scrolled` on any other route, so every other route had a body locked to `overflow: hidden`: `PageDown`, Space and the arrow keys did nothing, and on a phone a finger swipe could not scroll at all (checked in real Chrome with a Pixel 5 touch swipe on `/terms` and `/contact`: scrollY stayed 0). The mouse wheel appeared to work only because the smooth-scroll library (Lenis) scrolls with script. That covers the legal pages, contact, sign-in, 404 and, from the same layout, very likely the creator tip page and dashboard (those need a database to render, so they were not driven here). The three rules now start `body:has(.page-content):not(.page-scrolled)`; `.page-content` is only on the home page (`src/app/page.tsx`), so only the home page has a title screen. No JavaScript is involved and nothing else changed.
- New `test/page-scroll.spec.ts`: on `/terms`, `/privacy`, `/pricing`, `/contact`, `/signin` and a 404 the body is not `overflow: hidden`; `/terms` and `/privacy` scroll with `PageDown`, and on the phone project with a real touch swipe; and the home page still starts locked (body `overflow: hidden`, content opacity 0). Against the old CSS 18 of these fail; with the fix they pass (2 skipped: the swipe tests on the desktop project).
- Verified: eslint and tsc clean; full suite 322 passed and 2 skipped, twice in a row.
- The rest of this step (owner chose "keep the title screen, make it safe", 2026-10-29), now done and described in `DESIGN.md`: (1) **CSS failsafe** (`globals.css`, `--title-failsafe`, 15 s): if the page's scripts fail or are blocked nothing ever adds `page-scrolled`, so after 15 s the content shows, the splash overlay (`data-splash-overlay`) hides, the scroll lock lifts and every paused `.enter` and `.rise` element jumps to its end state; all of it only applies while the page is still on the title screen, so it stops the moment `page-scrolled` arrives. (An attempt to un-pause the entry animations through an animated custom property did not work: Chrome did not recompute `animation-play-state` on descendants. Each entry-animated element now gets a second animation that finishes it at the failsafe time; those rules repeat the `.enter` and `.rise` values, so keep them in step.) (2) **Tab into the hidden page ends the title screen** (`focusin` inside `.page-content`), so keyboard focus is never stranded on something invisible; the visible skip link is still the first Tab stop. (3) **A click or tap ends it** (`pointerdown`), and the hidden content has `pointer-events: none`, so an invisible link can no longer be followed by a stray click. (4) **A small cue** ("Scroll to continue", or "Swipe up to continue" on touch devices) fades in 1.4 s after the splash and out when the title screen ends.
- Tests: `test/title-screen-keys.spec.ts` now has 13 tests (added: Tab into the hidden page, a click on the hidden page does not follow a hidden link and ends the title, the cue appears and goes, entry animations wait through the title screen and then still play) and `test/title-screen-failsafe.spec.ts` blocks every script file and checks the page is revealed by the failsafe (shortened to 1 s through `--title-failsafe`). The CSS failsafe runs on real time, so the fake-clock tests switch it off. Against the previous commit the failsafe, Tab, hidden-click and cue tests fail on both projects. A new shared `test/helpers.ts` (`skipTitleScreen`) is used by the waitlist, stream-toggle and 320px approval-row tests: with the hidden page now unclickable, they had to wait out the title screen (4.4 to 8.7 s and 3.6 to 9.9 s), and under heavy load that reached the 30 s limit; pressing a key first, like a visitor, brings them back to their old speed. The title-screen spec also has a 90 s timeout and bigger clock steps, because running the splash forward at a load average above 60 had failed the earlier version in about one run in three (that includes the committed version from the previous step, which I had described as stable).
- Verified: eslint and tsc clean; full suite 332 of 332 on desktop and mobile Chrome, three runs in a row at load averages of 3, 19 and 110. Screenshots of the title screen at 1280 px and a Pixel 5 checked by eye (cue placement and wording). The failsafe's CSS was checked in Chrome only: the animations of `overflow`, `visibility` and `pointer-events` and the `:has()` selector are standard in current Chrome, Safari and Firefox, but Safari and Firefox were not run here (the webkit project cannot run in this setup).

## Current state (2026-10-29, updated after the order rate limit)

- **Owner action: run `db/migrations/0012_tip_order_attempts.sql` in Neon.** Until it is run the limit does nothing (see "fails open" below); tips are not affected either way.
- `createTipOrder` (`src/app/actions/tip.ts`) now limits how many tips one network can start: 60 per hour, counted before the Razorpay order is created, so a refused request costs no Razorpay call and no order rows. Same pattern as the waitlist and contact form (an HMAC of the IP address with `WAITLIST_SALT`, one hour of rows in `tip_order_attempts`, purged on each new tip and by the daily cron), with three differences for the payment path. (1) The limit is high on purpose: Indian mobile users share one address behind their carrier, and refusing a fan who is about to pay costs more than an unpaid order does; raise or lower `ORDER_LIMIT_PER_HOUR` in one place. (2) A per-network database lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`) makes the count exact, so a burst of parallel requests cannot all read "0 so far" before any is recorded; refused requests are not recorded, so a flood cannot keep real users on the same address locked out past the hour. (3) It fails open: if the check cannot run (migration not run, salt missing or short, no client address in the headers, a database error) it logs and lets the tip through, so the limiter can never be the reason a payment fails. If the database is down the order still cannot be saved and the fan is told to try again (the fail-closed change above). Fans over the limit see "Too many tips started from your network — please try again in a little while".
- Not covered: this limits one address, so a script spread over many addresses is not stopped (nothing here defends against that; Razorpay's own order limits and monitoring are the backstop). Behind Vercel the client address comes from `x-forwarded-for`, the same assumption the waitlist and contact form make.
- Verified: eslint and tsc clean; full suite 302 of 302, twice. The limiter SQL and the real migration file were run on an in-memory Postgres (PGlite, outside the repo): the first 60 allowed, the 61st refused, refused requests not recorded, other networks unaffected, the one-hour window slides, the purge only removes old rows, the migration runs twice. The real `createTipOrder` was run against a fake database and headers: under the limit saves the limiter row, order and attempt; at the limit returns the error with no Razorpay call and nothing saved; a broken limiter, a missing salt and a missing address each let the tip through and log (except the missing address); database down still fails closed. The advisory lock's effect under real concurrency was not tested (PGlite is one connection).

## Current state (2026-10-29, updated after the title-screen key fix)

- `GlobalBackground` (`src/components/GlobalBackground.tsx`) swallows the first scroll gesture on the title screen so it only dismisses the title (that stays). Three faults fixed: (1) Space and the arrows were swallowed whatever had focus, so a space typed in a field was lost and Space on a focused button did nothing; the handler now leaves them alone when focus is in an input, textarea, select, button, summary, contenteditable or a widget role, and the 5.2 s timer still ends the title screen; (2) the keydown listener had `{ once: true }`, which any key (Tab, a letter) used up, so a later Space scrolled the page under the title screen without dismissing it; (3) when the timer ended the title screen the three listeners stayed attached, so the user's first scroll key (or wheel or touch) afterwards was swallowed. A `detach()` now removes all three the moment the title screen goes, by any route.
- New `test/title-screen-keys.spec.ts` (5 tests, desktop and mobile). The title screen only lasts 5.2 s, so the page runs on Playwright's fake clock: time is paused until the test moves it (`runFor` through the splash, `fastForward` past the hold), which keeps the tests independent of machine load. An early version that waited in real time failed under parallel load, and a version that simulated the whole hold frame by frame made other tests time out; the fast-forward version passed the full suite twice.
- Verified: eslint and tsc clean; full Playwright suite 294 of 294 on chromium-desktop and chromium-mobile, twice in a row (1.1 min). Run against the old component the new spec fails 8 of 10 (all but "the first Space only dismisses the title screen", which is the behaviour kept). Also checked by hand in real Chrome before and after: field space, button Space, Tab then Space, first Space, first Space after the timer, first wheel after the timer.
- Gaps noted at the time (closed in the entry above): the touch path had no test of its own; a custom widget with none of the listed roles still had its first Space swallowed.

## Current state (2026-10-29, updated after the lost-capture fix)

- Owner decision (2026-10-29): fail closed. `createTipOrder` (`src/app/actions/tip.ts`) used to treat a failed database write as non-fatal and return the Razorpay order anyway, so a fan could pay for a tip with no row to credit and the webhook acknowledged it with nothing updated. Now the order and its payment attempt are saved in one transaction (`db.begin`) before the fan can pay; if that fails the fan sees "Service unavailable — try again shortly", the payment popup never opens, and the retry (fixed earlier today) works. The unused Razorpay order is harmless. The transaction also means a failure between the two inserts no longer leaves an order without its attempt.
- Webhook: a capture for an order with no `payment_attempts` row at all is now logged (`Razorpay capture with no matching payment attempt`, with payment id, order id and amount; no fan details). It still answers 200, because a retry cannot create the row and repeated failures can get the webhook switched off at Razorpay. With the fail-closed change this should not happen for orders made by the site; if the log line ever appears, reconcile that payment by hand. A duplicate webhook is still a quiet no-op.
- Verified: eslint and tsc clean, Playwright 284 of 284 (it builds first). `createTipOrder` was run unchanged against a fake database that can fail: healthy gives ok with both rows saved; database down gives an error and nothing saved; attempt insert failing gives an error and the order rolled back. The old version run through the same harness returns ok with the database down and leaves an order with no attempt. The two inserts, the rollback and the new lookup were also run as SQL on an in-memory Postgres (PGlite, outside the repo). Still no permanent test, for the same reason as before (needs a database).
- The `origin` remote was changed to `https://github.com/atulsharma568/Freetip.git` afterwards (see the entry above).

## Current state (2026-10-29, updated after the payment fixes)

- Razorpay webhook (`src/app/api/webhooks/razorpay/route.ts`): a fan can fail a payment and retry in the same popup, so one order sees several payments but has one `payment_attempts` row. A capture used to be dropped when a failure had already filled the row (`provider_payment_ref` was set), leaving a paid tip marked failed. Now `payment.captured` also replaces a row whose status is `failed`, and clears its failure columns; a duplicate capture still updates nothing, and `payment.failed` still only fills an empty row, so a late failure for an earlier payment cannot overwrite a capture. The tip order is not pulled back out of `refunded`, `disputed` or `closed`. The attempt row and the tip order now change in one transaction (`db.begin`), so a crash between the two statements no longer leaves the order behind (Razorpay's retry redoes both).
- Tip form (`src/app/[handle]/TipForm.tsx`): after any error the Pay button now works again (it looked enabled but `canPay` only allowed `idle`). Also fixed on the same path: a failed Razorpay script load left a dead `<script>` tag, so every retry hung on "Opening payment…"; a thrown server action or Razorpay constructor left the form stuck. Each now shows an error and allows a retry.
- Verified: eslint and tsc clean, production build clean, Playwright 284 of 284 on chromium-desktop and chromium-mobile. The webhook statements were run against an in-memory Postgres (PGlite, outside the repo) over 9 cases (fail then paid retry, late failure after capture, duplicate captures and failures, refunded order, rollback) plus a control showing the old SQL drops the retry. The Pay button was driven in a real Chrome on a temporary page (removed): before the fix the second click sent no request, after it sends one. There is no permanent test for either: both need a database or a tip page that renders without one.
- Still open from the review (the lost-capture one was fixed afterwards, see the entry above): the page is invisible until first scroll or 5 s; unauthenticated order creation has no rate limit.

## Current state (2026-10-25, updated after tests and policy wording)

Tests, accessibility and policy wording (2026-10-25):

- Stale tests fixed. `/signup` is now the Google sign-up page ("Get early access"), and the waitlist form lives only in the home page's closing section (handle and email; the channel field is gone). `test/waitlist.spec.ts` now tests the home form (invalid email, handle filtering, no-database message) plus a `/signup` test for the Google button and terms and privacy links. The metadata test expects the new title and description. The `/kabirplays` skip-link failure was a race, not a missing link: that route renders the 404 page, whose content streams in after `domcontentloaded`, so the test now waits for the link before the first Tab. Result: 102 of 102 on desktop Chrome and 102 of 102 on Pixel 5 Chrome, desktop run three times in a row.
- Regression found and fixed: `RevealWords` used SplitText `aria: "auto"`, which put an `aria-label` on plain `<p>` elements (ARIA forbids it) and dropped Lighthouse accessibility from 100 to 96 on the live site. Now `aria: "none"`; local Lighthouse accessibility is back to 100.
- Live site before that fix (Lighthouse 12 run locally against https://tipsite.vercel.app, mobile emulation): performance 83, accessibility 96, best practices 100, SEO 63 (SEO is the intended block on search engines until the domain is live). FCP 2.4 s, LCP 3.4 s, TBT 150 ms, CLS 0, Speed Index 5.0 s. PageSpeed Insights' own API refused the request (the keyless daily quota is used up), so this is Lighthouse, not PageSpeed, and its scores differ a little from PageSpeed's. Performance was 86 on 2026-10-16. After this commit deployed, a second local Lighthouse run on the live site gave performance 93, accessibility 100, best practices 100, SEO 63 (FCP 2.2 s, LCP 2.2 s, TBT 130 ms, CLS 0.011, Speed Index 4.5 s), so the 83 above was run-to-run variance; treat single runs as roughly plus or minus 10 and use PageSpeed Insights (with an API key or in the browser) for a number to quote.
- Policy wording (owner direction: do what the competitors do; refunds are not offered). Competitor pattern, from their public terms and refund pages read on 2026-10-25: SuperTips says SuperBits are "final and non-refundable under normal circumstances", refunds only a verified platform error, bans chargebacks, may "delay, withhold, or reverse settlements" on suspected fraud, and pays creators "on a regular schedule" with no day count; StreamTipz announces a "Strict No-Refund Policy", has no payout system (tips go straight to the creator's own merchant account), and prohibits abusive chargebacks. Changes: `/refunds` now says plainly "We don't offer refunds on tips" with a list of reasons that don't qualify (change of mind, wrong amount or creator, blocked or unread message, creator offline), and the old "When we refund" list is retitled "Payment errors we put right" (charged twice, debited but failed, different amount, our technical error, unauthorised use), with a note that arrival depends on the bank, UPI app and payment partner. Terms, delivery page and FAQ agree: payouts go to the registered bank account or UPI ID (the dashboard collects a UPI ID) on a regular schedule, no promised day; new FAQ "Can a tip be refunded?". Kept as they were: holds on suspected fraud or chargebacks, repeat-chargeback suspension, the 7-day report window and the 24 h / 5 day / 2 day / 5 to 7 day timeline (these defaults still need the owner's confirmation and a lawyer).
- Not done, needs the owner: operator details in `src/lib/legal.ts` (legal name, business type, registered address, court city, support email, phone, hours, Grievance Officer name, designation and email, GSTIN) are the owner's own facts and were not invented; `LEGAL_DRAFT` stays true until a lawyer has reviewed. The 18+ rule is kept (the competitors state no age rule; it is the safer default). `docs/hindi-copy.md` was already behind the live FAQ and was not updated. The payout engine (initiating payouts, settlements, reconciliation) is not built.

## Current state (2026-10-25, updated after the last five polish ideas)

Five polish items from the ideas list, own code (2026-10-25):

- Fee switcher (`Money.tsx`, `feeBreakdown` in `src/lib/legal.ts`): "Try a tip" radios (₹50, ₹100, ₹500, ₹1,000) re-work the worked example from our 5% and the 2.36% processing charge, rounded to the paise (₹100 gives ₹92.64, same as `FEES.example`). Capped below ₹2,000 on purpose, where the FAQ's extra 0.4% UPI charge starts. Real radio inputs, so arrow keys work; the figures settle with the existing quick pop.
- Link hover variants (`globals.css`): `.link-line` (draws from the left, footer company and policy links), `.link-center` (grows from the middle, defined for reuse), `.link-fill` (lime block rises, text turns dark; footer Product links). Links inside `.legal` prose now use the centre-out line over a grey base line. All instant under reduced motion.
- Confetti (`TipForm.tsx` `SuccessScreen`): 40 small lime, white and grey pieces burst from the amount after the note flies off, then fade. None under reduced motion; hidden in the markup so nothing shows without JS.
- Word-by-word reveal (`src/components/motion/RevealWords.tsx`, GSAP SplitText words, aria label kept): used on the Why lead paragraph and the fee paragraph. Skipped under reduced motion.
- Full-screen phone menu (`Nav.tsx`): below 1024px a round button opens a full-screen panel with the five section links and Sign in / Sign up (or Sign out / Dashboard). Locks the page behind it, closes on Escape, on a link click or when the screen grows to desktop, keeps Tab inside the header, returns focus to the button. Below 640px the header buttons moved into the menu, which fixes the 320px "Sign up" cut-off noted earlier.
- Verified 2026-10-29: tsc clean, eslint clean on the logo, production build clean; intro checked frame by frame at 1440, finished logo at 390, reduced motion static. Playwright suite: 284 of 284 pass on chromium-desktop and chromium-mobile using installed Chrome (the bundled Chromium cannot be downloaded here, so a config with `channel: "chrome"` was used from /tmp). - Not tried: the menu on a real phone and in Safari; confetti on a real payment (needs Neon and a Razorpay test payment); PageSpeed with these changes. Not built: the "Processing, Approved" tip story.

## Current state (2026-10-23, updated after overlay text-to-speech)

Text-to-speech on the OBS overlay (2026-10-23): built, not yet heard. It has not been tried against real Polly or inside real OBS (no AWS credentials exist yet, and the Polly setup wizard is gone from `/tmp`).

- How it works: the overlay API marks an alert `speech: true` when the rules allow it; the overlay then downloads `GET /api/overlay/[token]/speech/[id]` (an MP3) before showing the card, plays it with the card, and keeps the card up until the voice ends (+1.5 s). Any failure (speech off, engine down, slow network, 204) shows the alert silently.
- Rules (pure, `src/lib/tts-rules.ts`, `test/tts-rules.spec.ts`): only the fan's message is spoken, never the name. It needs `tts_enabled`, a tip of at least `users.tts_min_paise` (default ₹49, creator's "Read aloud from" setting), and a message that is approved, or pending with "approve before play" off. A rejected message is never spoken. Links and invisible characters are stripped, and the text is capped at the creator's message limit or 300 characters. The speech route re-checks every rule from the database and speaks only the stored text, only for a message shown in the last 10 minutes.
- Previously `approve_before_play` was saved but not used anywhere. It now gates speech. Shortcut: in `instant` mode the message still shows on screen before approval (existing behaviour); with approve-before-play on it is just never spoken.
- Engine: Amazon Polly, Kajal neural (`src/lib/tts.ts`), Devanagari text read as `hi-IN`, everything else `en-IN`. Roman-script Hindi is read with an English accent (known limit). Plain text, never SSML. Env vars are `POLLY_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `POLLY_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, optional `POLLY_REGION` (default `ap-south-1`), not `AWS_*`, which Vercel reserves. Without them alerts play silently. Swapping to Sarvam later only changes `synthesize()`.
- Browser note: a normal browser tab blocks sound until a click, so the voice is only heard in OBS (its browser source allows autoplay; tick "Control audio via OBS" to put it on the mixer). The `/overlay/preview` page does not speak.
- **To activate:** run `db/migrations/0010_tts_threshold.sql` in Neon **before or right after the deploy** (the dashboard and overlay queries read `tts_min_paise` and fail until it exists), create an IAM user allowed only `polly:SynthesizeSpeech`, add the two `POLLY_*` variables in Vercel, then test in real OBS.

## Current state (2026-10-23, updated after OBS alerts)

OBS alerts, visual only (2026-10-23): a creator gets one private overlay link for an OBS Browser source; captured tips play as alerts. Text-to-speech is the next slice (speech inside OBS's browser source is unreliable and needs its own testing).

- Layouts: one link, the layout follows the source size. 1080p (1920×1080) and 2K (2560×1440) are one 16:9 design drawn at their own size; vertical (1080×1920) is a stacked layout. Everything is sized from one unit (1% of the stage width, measured with a ResizeObserver), not viewport or container units, which older OBS browsers may lack. Reduced motion falls back to a fade.
- Alert modes (creator's choice, `users.alert_mode`, default `split`): `instant` plays everything at once; `split` plays name and amount at once and the message as its own card once approved; `approve` plays nothing until the message is decided. A rejected message is never shown (in `approve` mode the tipper is still thanked). Rules are pure and tested: `src/lib/alerts.ts`, `test/overlay-rules.spec.ts`.
- Delivery: the overlay polls `GET /api/overlay/[token]` every 4 s. One query finds the creator by token hash plus candidate tips; the endpoint hands out at most one alert and marks it shown in the same request (`alert_shown_at` / `message_shown_at`), so it plays once. (Superseded 2026-10-30: the hand-out is now a lease and the overlay confirms; see the top of this file.) Alerts older than an hour are dropped so a late-opened overlay does not replay old tips.
- Link security: 32 random bytes, only the SHA-256 hash is stored (`users.overlay_token_hash`), shown once, regenerating kills the old link. Overlay pages are `noindex`, `no-referrer`, disallowed in robots.txt; the API is `no-store`, 404s on a malformed or unknown token before any lookup beyond one indexed query.
- Dashboard: "Alerts on stream" tile (create or replace link, OBS sizes, preview link), "Messages to approve" queue (hidden in `instant` mode), alert-mode radio in settings. `/overlay/preview` shows sample alerts at all three sizes, no secret needed.
- `decideMessage` lives in `src/app/actions/messages.ts`, not `creator.ts`: a "use server" file that a server component imports is evaluated on the server, and `creator.ts` exports plain constants, which fails the build.
- **To activate:** run `db/migrations/0009_obs_alerts.sql` in Neon.
- Verified 2026-10-29: tsc clean, eslint clean on the logo, production build clean; intro checked frame by frame at 1440, finished logo at 390, reduced motion static. Playwright suite: 284 of 284 pass on chromium-desktop and chromium-mobile using installed Chrome (the bundled Chromium cannot be downloaded here, so a config with `channel: "chrome"` was used from /tmp). The webkit-iphone project cannot run here (no WebKit). ## Current state (2026-10-22, updated after payment flow)

Razorpay payment flow (2026-10-22, commit d49eaee): fans can now pay on an active creator's tip page.

- `src/app/actions/tip.ts`: `createTipOrder` server action — validates amount (≥ min, ≤ ₹1,00,000), fetches active creator by handle, calls `POST /v1/orders` on Razorpay, persists `tip_orders` + `payment_attempts` rows, returns `razorpayOrderId` and `razorpayKeyId` to the client.
- `src/app/[handle]/TipForm.tsx`: real payment flow. Phase machine: idle → creating → checkout → success | error. Razorpay checkout.js loaded lazily at pay time. Success screen shows paid amount and "Send another tip".
- `src/app/api/webhooks/razorpay/route.ts`: handles `payment.captured` and `payment.failed`. Idempotent via `payment_attempts.provider_payment_ref` UNIQUE constraint. Updates `tip_orders.status` and `payment_attempts` on capture or failure.
- **Razorpay test keys are in Vercel.** To test end-to-end: activate a creator in `/admin/creators`, visit their tip page, and tip with Razorpay's test card (4111 1111 1111 1111, any CVV/expiry). The webhook fires if the webhook URL is registered in Razorpay dashboard.
- Not yet built: OBS alerts, text-to-speech, creator payout initiation.

## Current state (2026-10-22, updated after Razorpay activation gate)

Razorpay activation gate (2026-10-22, commit e1a6c60): creator payout onboarding flow that gates tip page activation.

- `db/migrations/0008_payout_onboarding.sql`: adds `upi_vpa`, `legal_name` and `onboarding_state` (`not_started` → `submitted` → `active` | `rejected`) to users.
- `submitPayoutDetails` server action: validates legal name and UPI VPA (regex `name@bankhandle`), stores to DB, sets `onboarding_state = 'submitted'`. Only accepted when state is `not_started` or `rejected`.
- `PayoutOnboarding.tsx`: dashboard tile that branches by state — form → under-review panel → live. Pre-fills legal name from Google name, shows submitted UPI VPA back to creator.
- Dashboard: pending tile replaced with `PayoutOnboarding`.
- `setCreatorStatus` (admin): now syncs `onboarding_state` — approving → `active`, resetting to pending → `not_started`.
- `rejectCreatorOnboarding` (admin): sets `onboarding_state = 'rejected'`; creator can resubmit.
- Admin creators page: Payout column shows `onboarding_state` badge + UPI VPA; Reject button for submitted-but-pending creators.
- `POST /api/webhooks/razorpay`: HMAC-SHA256 signature verified with `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`; stubs for `payment.captured` and `payment.failed`.
- `.env.example`: documents `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID`, `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET`, `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`.
- **To activate:** run `0008_payout_onboarding.sql` in Neon. Add `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` to Vercel when the Razorpay account is created.

## Current state (2026-10-22, updated after security pass)

Security hardening (2026-10-22): confirmed live bypass of admin layout auth via RSC partial render — all 8 admin page.tsx files now call requireOwner() directly, independent of the layout; handle param validated against HANDLE_PATTERN before any DB query in [handle]/page.tsx; admin.ts args runtime-validated; auth.ts: email_verified required, fail-closed in production when DB missing, email unique-collision handled, access_type offline removed; next.config.ts: X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy headers on every route; cron route: timingSafeEqual for bearer secret; creator.ts: redirect moved after try block, generic error catch, expanded RESERVED set; db.ts: ssl: "require" explicit.

## Current state (2026-10-22, updated after creator onboarding slice)

Creator onboarding / public tip-page data (2026-10-22): two gaps that blocked creators from feeling fully onboarded are now closed.

1. **Custom display name**: new `display_name` column (migration `0007_display_name.sql`). Creators set a streamer name in dashboard settings (first field, above bio, 50-char limit). ## Current state (2026-10-22, updated)

Pre-launch landing page for a UPI-first tipping site for Indian YouTube live streamers, built with Next.js 16, Tailwind v4, GSAP, Lenis and one React Bits component.

Page sections, in order:
- Hero: headline, subline, a call to action, a hands-handoff animation and a self-playing sample tip card that starts after the hands finish.
- How it works: four steps (claim, share, go live, get paid) down a lime line that draws as you scroll, each with a labelled sample card (see 2026-10-25 entry).
- On your stream: a labeled sample OBS alert whose message lights up word by word, like text-to-speech reading it.
- Why Tipsite: no fan account or email, optional anonymous tips, UPI + card in one flow, a clearly labelled sample QR-first sharing treatment, and safer text-to-speech with screening and creator approval in the planned flow.
- Payments and fees: UPI, cards and "We take 5% of every tip."
- FAQ and a closing section: a money orbit around the glitching wordmark, then the waitlist form (optional handle with the typing handle field, plus email).
- `/signup` and `/signin` use Google OAuth (next-auth v5). On sign-in the creator's YouTube channel is fetched and saved. `/dashboard` is a protected early-access holding page. Nav switches between Sign in/Sign up (logged out) and Dashboard/Sign out (logged in).
- **Needs Vercel env vars before auth works:** `AUTH_SECRET`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET` and `db/schema-users.sql` run in Neon.
- Deployed (2026-10-16) at https://tipsite.vercel.app on Vercel, with the server pinned to Singapore (`sin1` in `vercel.json`) next to the Neon Postgres database (AWS Singapore).
- A real waitlist: a Server Action saves entries to Neon Postgres with server-side checks, a bot trap, a per-network rate limit and a daily clean-up job. It needs `DATABASE_URL`, `WAITLIST_SALT` and `CRON_SECRET` in Vercel and `db/schema.sql` run once (see "Waitlist setup" below); without them the form says the waitlist isn't working.
- A share image for link previews, an iPhone home-screen icon, a 404 page that explains creator links aren't live yet, `robots.txt` and `sitemap.xml`.
- Policy and company pages: `/terms`, `/privacy`, `/refunds`, `/delivery`, `/contact` (with Grievance Officer), `/pricing` and `/about`, linked from a new footer on every page. They are drafts: operator details are `[placeholders]` in `src/lib/legal.ts`, and the text needs legal review before launch.

Creator tip page and handle setup are now built (commit a1ea23e, 2026-10-22). Not built yet: Razorpay payment processing, real KYC onboarding flow, OBS alerts, text-to-speech. The first approved implementation slice is a read-only owner-finance foundation: forward ledger schema, server-side owner authorization, protected `/admin` pages and honest empty states. The product name "Tipsite" and the domain `tipsite.in` are placeholders.

## Owner-finance foundation (2026-10-22)

- Scope: read-only owner finance foundation; no Razorpay payment execution, webhooks, refunds or payouts yet.
- `db/migrations/0001_finance_foundation.sql`: double-entry ledger, tip orders, payment attempts, fee lines, refunds, disputes, settlements, creator payouts, reconciliation, audit events. Append-only triggers on ledger and audit tables.
- `db/migrations/0002_finance_gap_fill.sql`: fills four gaps in 0001 — versioned `platform_fee_policies` (seeds 5% launch rate), `provider_events` for idempotent Razorpay webhook ingestion, `settlement_items` linking payments to settlement batches, `tip_orders.anonymous` flag, and `updated_at` columns + triggers on mutable tables.
- `src/lib/owner-auth.ts` + `owner-auth-core.ts`: server-side owner guard using `OWNER_GOOGLE_IDS` env var; unauthenticated → redirect to `/signin`, non-owner → 404.
- `/admin` layout + pages: protected admin shell with nav; sub-pages (`/admin/finance`, `/admin/audit`, `/admin/reconciliation`) are honest empty states — no fabricated totals.
- Splash/scroll-lock isolated from `/admin`: `SplashScreen` sets `data-splash="true"` on body; `SmoothScroll` only freezes Lenis when that flag is present. Admin pages scroll freely.
- **To activate:** run `0001` then `0002` in Neon SQL Editor; add `OWNER_GOOGLE_IDS=<your-google-id>` to Vercel env vars.
- Competitor findings from the public REA pass are maintained in `docs/competitor-findings.md`.
- `db/migrations/0001_finance_foundation.sql` now defines the forward-only finance tables, ledger accounts, reconciliation records and append-only history triggers. It does not connect to Razorpay.
- `src/lib/owner-auth.ts`, `src/app/admin/` and the owner route test now provide a server-side owner allowlist and read-only admin shell. Unauthenticated `/admin` requests redirect to `/signin`; the owner allowlist fails closed when unset.
- Finance, reconciliation and audit pages currently show empty states only. No payment, refund, settlement or payout action is exposed.

Tip button shows a "payments coming soon" message since Razorpay is not yet integrated.

## Implemented in the latest changes

Fee correction and UPI MDR FAQ (2026-10-21, commit cd2c5a9):

- `src/lib/legal.ts`: gateway fee corrected to Razorpay standard 2% + 18% GST = 2.36% effective; worked example updated from ₹92.50 → ₹92.64 on a ₹100 tip.
- `src/components/sections/Faq.tsx`: "What does it cost?" answer updated to match; new FAQ item "Is there an extra charge on large tips?" explains the govt-mandated 0.4% UPI MDR on tips above ₹2,000 (comes out of creator payout, not the fan's tip).

TTS decision (2026-10-21):
- Engine: Amazon Polly, Kajal Neural voice (bilingual Hindi+English). Free tier: 1M chars/month for 12 months. After month 12: switch to Sarvam AI Bulbul V4 Flash (better Hinglish, ~6x cheaper at ₹200/10,000 alerts vs Polly's ₹1,350). Switch is a single API call change.
- Rejected: Web Speech API and Edge TTS (poor Hinglish), ElevenLabs (mispronounces Roman-script Hindi), Sarvam now (only 100 free credits on signup, not enough for MVP).
- TTS is a threshold feature, not default-on: creator sets a minimum tip amount (e.g. ₹49+) and a message character cap (e.g. 100 chars). Tips below the threshold get a silent alert only. This controls API cost and mirrors how StreamElements handles it.
- Setup wizards: Razorpay KYC at /tmp/razorpay-kyc-wizard.sh, Polly at /tmp/polly-setup-wizard.sh.
- Not built yet — decisions recorded here for when the tip page is built.

Payment provider decision (2026-10-21): Razorpay confirmed as the payment provider. Cashfree was considered (lower headline rate) but Razorpay wins on zero annual fee, higher UPI success rate (~93% vs ~80–85%) and lower total cost at early volumes. Cashfree breaks even only above ~₹2 crore/year GMV.

Google OAuth live (2026-10-21):

- `AUTH_SECRET`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET` added to Vercel production env vars.
- `db/schema-users.sql` run in Neon — `users` table created.
- Google Cloud Console: OAuth 2.0 client created, YouTube Data API v3 enabled, redirect URI `https://tipsite.vercel.app/api/auth/callback/google` added, owner added as test user.

- `AUTH_SECRET` — generate with `openssl rand -base64 32`

- `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET` — matching client secret
